### T-R4.3 — Console display composites and idle-aware polling
**Depends on:** R1 primitives
**Touches:** `web/src/components/shared/stats/{KpiTile,StatStrip,ProgressBar,Meter}.tsx` (new), `web/src/components/shared/charts/BarChart.tsx` (new), `web/src/components/shared/{IconTile,Callout,LockNotice,EventList,SplitPane,Waveform}.tsx` (new), `web/src/hooks/useIdlePolling.ts` (new), `web/src/layouts/shell/contentWidth.ts` (new), `web/src/features/attempts/components/Monitor.tsx`, `web/src/features/integrity/components/Timeline.tsx`, `web/tests/support/viewport.ts` (a numeric form, `viewport(800)`)
**Size:** M
**Delivered in parts:** three, each one branch and one pull request
**T-R4.3a:** the two shell hooks, `useIdlePolling` (with `useRefetchOnResume`) and the content-width store (`useContentWidthAtLeast`), and the numeric form of the `viewport()` test helper. Depends on nothing in R4
**T-R4.3b:** `KpiTile`, `StatStrip`, `ProgressBar`, `Meter`, `BarChart`, `IconTile`, `Callout`, `LockNotice`, the generic event list. Depends on nothing in R4
**T-R4.3c:** `SplitPane`, `Waveform`. Depends on nothing in R4; it takes `useColumnWidth`'s tests as its model
**As built:**
- **T-R4.3a: beyond the list,** the helper `web/tests/support/contentWidth.ts` and three test
  files: `web/tests/units/shared/idle-polling.test.tsx` and, in the new folder
  `web/tests/units/shell/` that T-R4.4 fills, `content-width.test.tsx` and
  `viewport-helper.test.ts`. `monitor.test.tsx` and the integrity `timeline.test.tsx` gained
  cases.
- **T-R4.3a: `useIdlePolling(ms: number, enabled = true): number | false`** is a query's
  `refetchInterval`: `ms` while `enabled`, the document is visible and the user is active, else
  `false`. It is called on the line above `useQuery`, and `refetchIntervalInBackground: false`
  stays.
- **T-R4.3a: activity is a `pointerdown`, `pointermove`, `keydown`, `wheel` or `touchstart` on
  `window` within `IDLE_AFTER_MS` (600 000).** One store for every consumer: the first attaches
  one set of listeners and one timer, the last removes them, an input only writes a timestamp,
  and a consumer re-renders when idleness or visibility flips, never on a mouse move. The
  listeners are passive and on the capture phase, so a handler that stops propagation (a row
  menu does) cannot hide an input.
- **T-R4.3a: mounting counts as activity through the store's first consumer.** The ten minutes
  start when the store attaches. A consumer that joins an idle store answers `false` until an
  input or a return to the tab, so a mount nobody caused cannot wake every poll; a disabled
  consumer attaches nothing.
- **T-R4.3a: `useRefetchOnResume(refetch: () => unknown, enabled = true): void` refetches at
  once when the user comes back:** once at the first input after idleness and once when a hidden
  document becomes visible, never on mount, because `enabled` changed, or on an input from a user
  who never left. The query client has `refetchOnWindowFocus: false`, so without it a monitor
  watched without input would show data up to ten minutes old for one more interval after the
  teacher's first mouse move. TanStack restarts the interval from that refetch.
- **T-R4.3a: becoming visible counts as activity.** A tab that comes back, after however long,
  refetches once and polls for another ten minutes; the first input after it does not refetch.
  Somebody brought the tab back, and without this a teacher who returns with the keyboard or on
  a tablet would read a roster that froze at the moment of return.
- **T-R4.3a: the old detail page's `monitor.polling` sentence is untrue for an idle teacher.**
  `AssignmentDetailPage` still prints "Tự cập nhật 15 giây/lần" on every open assignment, and the
  monitor under it now stops after ten minutes without input. The deck draws no such sentence:
  T-R4.25 removes it, or draws the paused state from `live && refetchInterval === false`, and
  changes the two tests that wait on it (`web/tests/units/assignments/detail.test.tsx` and
  `web/tests/e2e/author-and-publish.live.spec.ts`) and `Monitor`'s doc comment with it.
- **T-R4.3a: `Monitor` and the integrity `Timeline` switched,** four lines each: the import,
  `const refetchInterval = useIdlePolling(POLL_MS, live)`, the shorthand in the query, and
  `useRefetchOnResume(<query>.refetch, live)`. The import pages' polls (`imports/availability.ts`
  and the three pages) are left to T-R4.36 to T-R4.38, and the student's `AssignmentIntroPage`
  keeps its own: a student waiting for a test to open is not idle.
- **T-R4.3a: the content width is the registered element's border-box width minus its computed
  horizontal padding** (`offsetWidth − padding-left − padding-right`), which is the deck's `tw`.
  A vertical scrollbar sits inside the border box, so its appearing moves no threshold and no
  hysteresis is needed. The padding is read at every measure, because it changes at 768.
- **T-R4.3a: `contentWidth.ts` exports three things.** `registerContentElement(element | null)`
  is the ref of the shell's `<main>` (one element at a time, `null` unregisters; measured at
  once and on every `ResizeObserver` callback, observed with `box: "border-box"`).
  `useContentWidthAtLeast(px)` is a boolean. `useContentBand(thresholds)` counts how many of an
  ascending array the width meets, one subscription for a table's whole column set. The
  snapshot is the boolean or the count, so a consumer re-renders only when its own answer changes.
- **T-R4.3a: wide when unregistered.** With no element (a unit test, a page in the old shell, a
  server render) every threshold is met and the band is `thresholds.length`. Without
  `ResizeObserver` the width is measured once and stays. A unit test that renders the shell is
  not unregistered: its `<main>` measures 0 in jsdom, so every threshold is unmet unless the
  test calls `contentWidth(px)`.
- **T-R4.3a: a page that mounts in the same commit as its shell renders "wide" once.** `<main>`'s
  ref attaches after that first render, and the consumer corrects itself when it subscribes
  (`content-width.test.tsx` pins `[true, false]`). T-R4.4 can keep that frame off the screen by
  rendering the outlet once `<main>` is registered.
- **T-R4.3a: two test helpers.** `contentWidth(px)` (`web/tests/support/contentWidth.ts`) registers
  a stand-in `<main>` of that content width and a `ResizeObserver` that fires only when told,
  returns `{ resize(next) }`, and takes both away when the test finishes; it is called inside a
  test or a `beforeEach`. A `<main>` the tree under test registers in the stand-in's place (a
  rendered shell) has the same width and follows `resize`, whether the helper ran before the
  render or after it. `viewport()` also takes a number of CSS pixels: `(min-width: Npx)`
  matches at N and above, `(max-width: Npx)` at N and below, a query with both needs both, any
  other query is false. `viewport(800)` is "the sidebar shows and side panels are sheets",
  `viewport(1050)` "wide, below the search button's 1100". `"phone"` and `"desktop"` answer as
  before.
- **T-R4.3a: `monitor.test.tsx` kept its four cases and changed two lines of scaffolding.** Its
  `setVisibility` dispatches inside `act`, since a visibility change now re-renders the monitor,
  and its `afterEach` unmounts before it restores visibility, since a return to the tab now sends
  a request and that one would land in the next case's count.
- **T-R4.3b: beyond the list,** `stats/progress.ts` (`percent`, `thresholdTone`, `shares`),
  `charts/bars.ts` (`barHeights`, `labelShown`), `tests/units/shared/{stats,bar-chart,notices}.test.tsx`
  and the harness cases `tests/support/deck-harness/cases/r4-03b.tsx`. Every part is new, so its
  classes are plain, the same on and off a deck surface, with radii written as lengths
  (`rounded-[0.5rem]`; `rounded-md` is 6px off a deck surface). No dependency and no token.
- **T-R4.3b: `KpiTile` is a link when it has somewhere to go.** `{ label, icon, tone: "warning" |
  "danger" | "success" | "muted", value, hint, live? }` and either `{ to, action }` or neither: the
  types refuse one without the other. With `to` the whole tile is a react-router `Link`, named by
  its label, value, hint and action, whose border takes `--ring` under the pointer. Without `to`
  it is a `<div>` with the same frame, label, icon, value and hint: no role, no tab stop, no hover
  and no action. The deck's fixtures always have a destination, so that state is not drawn. The
  icon is the solid tone, not the ink, and a label that does not fit ends in an ellipsis.
  Both linked and plain tiles carry `group/kpi`: the composed `LiveDot` pauses while that
  tile is hovered or contains focus, resumes afterward and remains static under reduced motion.
  The pause utilities are important (`!`): `qz-live-dot`'s unlayered animation shorthand
  otherwise resets play state over normal layered utilities. This adds no tab stop or
  interactive hover styling to the plain tile and changes no shared CSS. Native border feedback is 150ms ease.
  A screen whose destination is conditional spreads it, `{...(to ? { to, action } : {})}`:
  `to={undefined}` does not compile (`to?: never` under `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`).
- **T-R4.3b: the tile's line heights.** The deck's tile is a `<button>`, so its rows have the
  browser's `normal`: the label row (13px) and the hint row (12.5px) are `leading-4`, 16px, and
  the value keeps the deck's own `leading-[1.15]`. A tile with one-line rows is 112.5px high. The
  rows are `<div>`s, so the link's name reads with a space between them.
- **T-R4.3b: `StatStrip`** takes `items: readonly { label, value, suffix?, tone?: "default" |
  "danger" }[]` and renders a `<dl>`. It is four columns when `useContentWidthAtLeast(640)` and two
  below; it never reads the viewport, so it changes when the sidebar collapses, and with no content
  area registered it is four. The value is `text-stat` on `leading-normal`; `danger` is
  `--danger-ink` on the value and the suffix stays muted. It is drawn for four items.
- **T-R4.3b: `ProgressBar`** takes `{ value, max? (100), label, size?: 6 | 8, tone?: "accent" |
  "danger" | "warning" | "success", cap?: "square" | "round", className? }`. It is a progressbar
  named by `label` whose `aria-valuenow` is the whole percentage, which is also the fill's width:
  `percent(value, max)` rounds, stays within 0 to 100 and gives 0 for a `max` of zero or less. The
  cap is square by default, clipped by the track (the Assignments list, item analysis);
  `cap="round"` is the Dashboard's. `thresholdTone(percent)` is danger below 40, warning below 65,
  else success. It is built of spans, so it can sit in a link, and the caller gives it its width.
- **T-R4.3b: `Meter`** takes `{ label, valueText, max, parts: readonly { key, value, tone: "accent" |
  "info" | "warning" | "danger" }[], className? }`. It is a meter named by `label`; `aria-valuenow`
  is the sum of the parts, never more than `max`, and `aria-valuetext` is `valueText`. A part's
  width is its share of `max` to two decimal places (`shares`), and a part that would run past the
  end of the track is cut there. A part that is negative or not a number counts as 0 in the value
  and in the fill alike; a `max` that is not a number is not guarded.
- **T-R4.3b: `BarChart`** takes `{ caption, columns: readonly [string, string], data: readonly {
  key, label, value, title }[], labelEvery? (1), className? }`. Bars and labels are `aria-hidden`
  and each column keeps its `title`. The text alternative is a `<table>` (the caption, two column
  headers, a row per item) inside an `sr-only` box: a table ignores a 1px height, and bare it
  would add its own to the page's scroll. The chart's root is `relative`: the box is absolutely
  positioned, and with nothing positioned above it its containing block is the page, so it would
  not scroll with `<main>` and would lengthen the page instead (T-R4.1a met the same thing in the
  table's menu header). Heights are whole percentages of the largest value (`barHeights`: all 0
  when nothing is above 0), and the last bar is the accent. `labelEvery` 2 draws indexes 1, 3, 5
  (`labelShown`) and an undrawn label keeps its slot. Heights transition for 200ms with native ease
  and no transition under reduced motion. No chart library.
- **T-R4.3b: `IconTile`** takes `{ icon, size?: 28 | 30 | 32 | 34 | 36 | 40 | 42 | 44 (32), tone?:
  "neutral" | "info" | "success" | "warning" | "danger" | "accent", className? }` and is
  `aria-hidden`. A size carries the deck's radius and icon size. The icon's size is set from the
  square (`[&>svg]:size-*`), so a caller restates it, or the radius, in `className`; the bell's
  muted row passes `text-muted-fg`.
- **T-R4.3b: `Callout`** takes `{ tone?: "neutral" | "info" | "warning" | "danger" | "success",
  size?: "sm" | "md" | "lg", icon?, lead?, children, actions?, onDismiss?, announce?, className? }`.
  `lg`, the default, is padding 12/14, radius 10, gap 10, 13px on 1.55 and a 16px icon; `md` is
  10/12, radius 8, gap 8, 13px on 1.5, 15px; `sm` is 8/10, radius 8, gap 8, 12.5px on 1.5, 14px.
  It has no role unless `announce` (`role="alert"`). `actions` are items of the callout's own row,
  which then wraps, with the text at `flex: 1 1 280px`; `null` and `false` count as no actions, so
  a screen may write `actions={can && <Button />}`. `onDismiss` adds the 24px button, and with
  both the text keeps the 280px basis (the deck draws no callout with both).
- **T-R4.3b: what a screen passes to `Callout`.** Line 1690 (T-R4.36): `icon={ShieldCheck}`, `lead`
  and `className="gap-3 [&>svg]:mt-px [&>svg]:size-[1.0625rem]"`. Line 1978 (T-R4.30):
  `tone="warning"`, `icon={PencilLine}`, `lead`, the two buttons as `actions` and
  `className="text-ui items-center gap-3 rounded-xl leading-normal [&>svg]:mt-0 [&>svg]:size-[1.0625rem]"`.
  Line 2046's muted icon: `[&>svg]:size-[0.9375rem] [&>svg]:text-muted-fg`. `className` is merged
  last and the icon's size, colour and margin are set from the root, so each is one class.
- **T-R4.3b: the callout's icon sits on the first line of the text (DG-134).** Lines 1690 and
  1708 set no `align-items`, so the deck's icon-font glyph stretches and lands at the middle of a
  text that wraps. The deck's other notes with the same 2px nudge say `flex-start`, and that is
  what is built; a screen that wants the middle passes `items-center`.
- **T-R4.3b: `LockNotice` is not dashed.** `{ reason, label? }` is deck line 360's inline marker: a
  13px lock and "Đã khoá" / "Locked", 12px, muted, on one line. The Teacher deck draws no dashed
  lock: the dashed note is the Student result page's and stays there. The reason is the `title`
  and a visually hidden sentence after the word, and the marker takes no tab stop. The marker is
  `relative`, as the chart's root is, so the hidden sentence scrolls with it.
- **T-R4.3b: `EventList`** takes `{ label, items: readonly { key, tone: "info" | "danger" |
  "success" | "warning" | "neutral", text: ReactNode, time }[], className? }`: an `<ol>` named by
  `label`. The dot is `aria-hidden` (`neutral` is `--border`), no line joins the dots, the last
  item keeps its 14px, and an empty list renders nothing.
- **T-R4.3b: not built.** Loading, empty and error states (the screens'), the grading card's 3px
  strip (T-R4.28 passes a class), the Dashboard's Today and activity lists and the attempt sheet's
  score tiles (their screens' markup), and a hover on the dismiss button (the deck draws none).
  The two keys are `display.locked` and `display.dismiss`, in a namespace before `monitor`.
- **T-R4.3b: independently compared in a browser.** QA covered 80 distinct non-easing cases
  and 240 fixture states at 360/768/1024/1280/1440 in both themes, including sidebar-derived
  widths and the 639/640px StatStrip boundary. The four original native-easing failures were
  retained and pass after the two scoped corrections on `46b81552`; chart intermediate and
  settled frames, 150/200ms timing, 1600ms pulse pause/resume and visible focus were measured.
  The narrow retest first passed 19/20; diagnostics passed 3/6 and 5/6. Failed strict
  zero-all-effects checks record a single permitted 0.01ms global color transition while
  the reduced dot remains opaque with animation-name none and no continuous pulse effect.
  Its exact trigger is unresolved; no failure was relabelled a pass or predicate relaxed.
  These static-title composites have no deck marquee trigger. Shared CSS and LiveDot stayed
  unchanged. Only `stat-strip` and the two charts change with width.
- **T-R4.3c: `SplitPane`** takes `label`, `first`, `second`, `unit`, `defaultSize`, `min`,
  `max`, optional `minSecond`, `step`, `storageKey`, `handle`, `split` and the root/first/second
  class names. It adds no card styling and imports no legacy column or width-store code.
- **T-R4.3c: pixels and percentages differ.** Pixels default to 16px key steps and use CSS
  `clamp` so the second pane retains its minimum as the row shrinks without an observer;
  interaction ceilings measure the row less the grip's 14px and `minSecond`. Percentages
  default to 2-point steps and use the pointer's absolute position within the measured row.
- **T-R4.3c: both pane wrappers retain their identity.** A caller stacks by passing `split`
  false and its own gap, never by unmounting a pane. T-R4.31a must use both halves of the
  builder's rule: `useMediaQuery("(min-width: 768px)") && useContentWidthAtLeast(594)`.
- **T-R4.3c: both handles are focusable vertical separators** with current/min/max values,
  arrow keys, Home/End and double-click reset; the grip gains these beyond the deck (DG-36).
  The 14px gutter holds a sticky 4×40px grip; the overlapping line is 9px with -5/-4px margins.
  Grip background feedback uses 150ms native ease and is static under reduced motion; the
  line and waveform states are instantaneous in normal motion; native comparison is recorded below.
- **T-R4.3c: storage is read once on mount,** finite values clamped, absent/invalid values
  defaulted, and all access guarded. A successful release writes once, keys write each size,
  and reset removes the key. Cancel, capture loss, stacking and unmount restore capture and
  the body's prior cursor/selection; canceled gestures keep their local size without writing.
- **T-R4.3c: `Waveform`** takes `seed`, optional `progress` and `className`; 28 decorative
  bars use the deck's numeric seed formula or stable 32-bit FNV-1a asset-id hash. Progress
  clamps to 0–1 and rounds the foreground bar count; the other bars use `--ring`. Default
  height is 44px, with caller height overrides, no role, label, tab stop or audio integration.
- **T-R4.3c: scope and strings.** No horizontal split, Enter collapse or audio-derived bars
  are built. `splitPane.hint` is inserted after `timeline` in both locales. The four harness
  cases show the grip, stacked panes, line and numeric waveform rows; playback is simulated.
  A fifth state fixture preserves two uncontrolled drafts across stacking and dragging.
- **T-R4.3c: independently compared in a browser.** Chromium measured twenty width/theme/
  sidebar references, genuine stored900 focus/first-key/reset, captured drag cleanup and
  stable drafts. Native grip feedback is 150ms ease with intermediate colours and no reduced
  transition; line focus has only the global 0.01ms reduced feedback before settling static.
  The 28 bars match the pinned heights and flex formula, with eleven active bars and instant
  toggles. QA records 53 executions, 43 passes and ten preserved scratch-probe failures,
  with no product defect, skip or automatic retry. Fixture frame and card widths are
  comparison context; future builder/review screens and passive continuous ARIA are not
  certified. No title marquee or continuous waveform animation belongs to this scope.
**Done when:**
- [x] `KpiTile` (a link when it has a destination, a plain card without one: icon, label, value,
      hint, action; optional R1 `LiveDot`),
      `StatStrip`, `ProgressBar` (6/8px; accent or threshold tone) and `Meter` exist
- [x] `BarChart`: tokens only, the latest bar in `--accent-c`, a `title` per bar, day labels, a
      visually hidden table; no chart library
- [x] `IconTile`, `Callout` and `LockNotice` (the deck's inline lock marker; the Teacher deck
      draws no dashed lock), and a generic `EventList` (dots by tone,
      text, time; the name `Timeline` is `features/integrity/components/Timeline.tsx`) exist
- [x] `SplitPane`: `role="separator"` with `aria-valuenow/min/max`, pointer drag, arrow keys,
      double-click to reset, clamping, and a `localStorage` key read and written inside
      try/catch
- [x] `Waveform`: 28 decorative bars seeded from an asset id, `aria-hidden`
- [x] `useIdlePolling(ms)` returns a `refetchInterval` only while the tab is visible and the
      user has given pointer or keyboard input, or come back to the tab, within the last ten
      minutes; it resumes on input or visibility, and `useRefetchOnResume` refetches once at
      that moment: the query client does not refetch on focus, so without it a returning
      teacher reads data up to ten minutes old for one more interval. `Monitor` and the
      integrity `Timeline` switch to it (PR-7). The three import pages' polls switch in
      T-R4.36 to T-R4.38
- [x] `useContentWidthAtLeast(px)` measures the element the layout registers (its border box
      minus its horizontal padding) when it is registered, then on every `ResizeObserver`
      callback, through `useSyncExternalStore`, so a consumer re-renders only when its
      threshold is crossed; it answers true when none is registered
- [x] Unit tests: split pane keyboard and clamp, storage failure tolerated, idle stop after ten
      minutes and resume on input (fake timers), chart text alternative present

### T-R4.4 — TeacherLayout
**Depends on:** T-R4.2a, T-R4.3a, T-R4.5b
**Touches:** `web/src/layouts/TeacherLayout.tsx` (new), `web/src/layouts/TeacherShell.tsx` (new), `web/src/layouts/TeacherSkeleton.tsx` (new), `web/src/layouts/shell/{Sidebar,TopBar,Breadcrumbs,WorkspaceSwitcher,PageHead}.tsx` (new), `web/src/layouts/teacherNav.ts` (new), `web/src/features/auth/AccountMenu.tsx`, `web/src/app/boot/handoff.ts`, `web/src/app/router.tsx`, `web/tests/units/shell/` (new), `web/tests/units/contract/teacher-nav.test.ts` (new), `web/tests/integration/router-chunks.test.ts` (pattern extended), `web/tests/units/student/shell.test.tsx`
**Size:** M
**As built:**
- **Touched beyond the list:** `app/boot/BootSplash.tsx` (one argument),
  `layouts/shell/{handle,crumbs,sidebarState,useNavCounts}.ts` (new),
  `tests/units/app/{teacher-routes,teacher-landing}.test.*` and
  `tests/support/deck-harness/cases/r4-04.tsx`. `AccountMenu.tsx` is three inner components now, to
  stay under the linter's complexity limit; its two earlier forms render as before, and
  `shell/account-foot.test.tsx` pins the student's byte for byte. `student/shell.test.tsx` needed
  no edit: its `/teacher` line passes no handle and stays true. No route gained a `handle`, so
  every teacher page still renders in `AdminLayout`. The Done-when lines below were corrected on
  the release branch while this was built (the whole trail, the any-of on Grading, both counts
  hidden at zero, the canary's pattern); the Touches line is the one still short.
- **The route contract (`layouts/shell/handle.ts`).** A rebuilt route declares
  `handle: { crumb, width?, sidebar? } satisfies TeacherHandle`. `crumb` is the WHOLE trail,
  ancestors first, each `{ key, to }`; the last entry is the page itself and has no `to`. The tree
  is flat, so a trail cannot be collected from matched parents. `width` is 1320 (the default),
  1080, 860, 720 or `"full"`. Only the LEAF match's handle is read, by the switch, the layout and
  the splash: a handle on the `imports` gate moves nothing.
- **`useCrumbs(tail)` (`layouts/shell/crumbs.ts`)** names a record: a tail of n `{ label, to? }`
  replaces the last n crumbs while the page is mounted. Null, undefined or an empty tail makes
  no registration, so an unnamed `PageHead` does not erase another caller's record title. A
  named caller clears its registration when it becomes empty or unmounts; the handle's labels
  then show while a record loads. The trail never changes length; a longer tail gives its last
  entries. It compares by value and does nothing outside the shell. The last crumb is the
  document title, "{page} · Quizzivy"; leaving the shell restores the title found at mount.
- **`PageHead` (`layouts/shell/PageHead.tsx`)** is `{ title, description?, actions?, back?,
  status?, crumb?, className?, children? }`: one `text-h1` h1, the 14px muted description, actions
  that wrap. `back` and `status` make the detail form (the link above the title, the pill beside
  it, a left column of `flex: 1 1 280px`); `children` follow the title. `crumb` calls `useCrumbs`
  with the title: a page uses that or its own `useCrumbs`, not both. `className` restates a class
  of the row.
- **The shell owns the page frame.** `<main>` is the scroller and the registered content element:
  padding 24/28/40, and 16/14/32 below 768, by CSS; inside it one wrapper, centred, with the
  handle's max width. It is registered in its ref callback and the outlet renders only after, so a
  page never renders a "wide" frame. On a change of the leaf route's id it returns to the top and
  takes focus unless focus is already inside it; a parameter change does neither. A page sets no
  padding and no max width.
- **Sidebar state (`layouts/shell/sidebarState.ts`).** `localStorage["quizzivy.sidebar"]` holds
  `"collapsed"` or `"expanded"`; absent or unreadable is expanded, and a write storage refused is
  kept for the page's life. It never collapses by itself. `handle.sidebar: "collapsed"` (T-R4.38)
  starts a route collapsed for the visit: the toggle works, the storage is neither read nor
  written, and leaving returns to the stored state. The deck collapses on entry and never
  restores; the plan's rule is built.
- **Below 768 the sidebar is a drawer** on Radix's dialog primitive (272px, `--z-sheet`), closed by
  default, with labels and badges whatever is stored. It closes on every navigation, the back
  button included, and does not reopen on a return to the entry it was opened at; focus then ends
  on `<main>`, and on the toggle after Esc. `#teacher-sidebar` carries `data-state`: `expanded`,
  `collapsed` or `open`.
- **Nav and gating (`layouts/teacherNav.ts`).** Eight destinations in the deck's order; a later
  module is absent. Each carries `operation` and `requires`, the `x-permission` of that list, and
  `contract/teacher-nav.test.ts` compares the two. Six need the workspace, Students
  `people.students.read`. Grading opens today's page, so it carries `listAttempts` and the any-of
  `[teaching.grading, teaching.attempts.intervene]`; T-R4.28 moves it to T-R4.14's queue and
  `teaching.grading`, and the contract test makes it. `activeNavId` lights by a prefix at a `/`,
  with the import pages under Tests and an attempt's page under Assignments; Settings lights
  nothing.
- **Counts (`layouts/shell/useNavCounts.ts`)** read `getDashboard` under `["admin-dashboard"]`, so
  today's six invalidations refresh the nav, every 60 s through `useIdlePolling` and once on
  return. Null while loading and after a failed request: no badge and no error. Zero draws no
  badge for either figure, as in the deck's script. T-R4.23 changes this one file to
  `GET /teacher/summary`. For an Admin the Assignments badge counts every teacher's open
  assignments until T-R4.19 narrows `getDashboard` to the caller's own, while the list it opens
  shows the Admin's own rows.
- **Top bar (`layouts/shell/TopBar.tsx`):** the toggle ("Collapse sidebar", "Expand sidebar",
  "Open menu" below 768), the trail (earlier crumbs are links, hidden below 768 by CSS), the
  search button (34, 200 and 260px by CSS at 768 and 1100) and the theme toggle. No bell: T-R4.45b
  adds it after the theme toggle, and today's `NotificationsButton` is not in the new shell. The
  palette is today's, mounted beside the deck root so it keeps its look until T-R4.6.
- **Account and workspace.** `AccountMenu` takes `sidebar?: "expanded" | "collapsed"` on its `deck`
  form: the foot row (a square avatar, the name, the role line "Admin" with the admin workspace
  and "Teacher" otherwise) with the student's menu opening above it; no "Admin console" row.
  `WorkspaceSwitcher` is a label for everyone, because `Sidebar` passes no destination; its
  button and "Switch workspace" menu are built and tested for a user with the admin workspace
  and a destination (R5). The mark is 18px high as drawn, under the kit's floor (DG-123), so the
  kit's development warning is expected.
- **Skeleton.** `TeacherLayout.tsx` registers `TeacherSkeleton`; its sidebar column shows from 768
  and is 60px, tile and icon blocks only, under a stored collapse (the Splash deck draws 248
  only). `skeletonFor(pathname, handle?)` draws the teacher frame only for a teacher handle, and
  `BootSplash` passes the leaf route's, so a page not yet rebuilt keeps fading straight onto
  itself.
- **A unit test of a page in the shell** uses `renderShell` and `serveDashboard` from
  `tests/units/shell/support.tsx` (one `dashboardBody` for every test) and calls
  `contentWidth(px)` first: jsdom's `<main>` measures 0, so without it every threshold is unmet.
  `viewport(800)` and `viewport(1050)` pin the states between thresholds, `viewport(360)` the
  drawer. Outside the shell `useCrumbs` is inert and the width answers "wide".
- **The chunk canary.** `ADMIN` gained `layouts/Teacher(Layout|Shell|Skeleton)`; the page pattern
  was already T-R4.5b's, and `layouts/shell/` is not named, because the splash loads `handle.ts`
  with the entry and `contentWidth.ts` is shared. The new case walks the static imports of the
  entry, of the chunk holding `layouts/TeacherShell` and of every chunk holding a route module,
  and finds no `@dnd-kit` module. On the unmodified tree, with `AdminLayout` as the shell, it was
  green with those roots: the library sits in the `OutlineTree` chunk, behind the builder's
  dynamic import.
- **Compared independently in the browser.** The production-build harness exercised the
  actual `TeacherLayout` at 360, 768, 1024, 1280 and 1440 in light and dark, expanded/collapsed
  or phone drawer states. The 93 geometry/function cases covered navigation, keyboard trap,
  focus restoration, list/detail headers, long Vietnamese and Splash; 10 production smoke/theme
  cases passed. Ten targeted animation retests approved `0b8a5e7d`: 200ms native ease-out for
  sidebar/drawer, 150ms menu motion, 1400ms skeleton shimmer paused on hover/focus, and static
  reduced motion. QA recorded the decided DG-122/DG-123/DG-22 departures and the deferred bell.
  Current page routes still use `AdminLayout`; no route was flipped in this task.
  T-R4.48 points the router at `TeacherLayout` and removes the switch, the
  handle condition in `handoff.ts` with `BootSplash`'s argument, and `AccountMenu`'s form without
  `deck`. T-R4.50 adds `quizzivy.sidebar` to 70 §6's storage list. Undrawn states built from the
  deck's parts: a collapsed item keeps its label as `title` and name, a count says what it counts,
  a long crumb ends in an ellipsis, a route with no crumb is titled "Quizzivy". Later page tasks
  must verify their actual route inside this independently compared shell.
**Done when:**
- [x] Sidebar 248px expanded, 60px collapsed (icons only, label in `title`, badges hidden), and a
      272px drawer below 768 with an overlay that closes on every navigation, traps focus and
      closes on Esc. It never collapses by itself; the collapsed state persists in
      `localStorage['quizzivy.sidebar']` inside try/catch
- [x] A route can ask for a transient collapse, which leaves the stored state alone: the review
      workspace (T-R4.38) collapses the sidebar on entry, and leaving restores the previous state
- [x] Nav, in R4: (no label) Dashboard; **Teaching** Assignments (muted count of live
      assignments), Grading (lime count of answers to grade), Classes, Students; **Content**

- [x] Each item carries the `x-permission` of the list operation it opens;
      `teacher-nav.test.ts` reads `api/openapi.yaml` and fails when they differ. A user sees

- [x] Top bar 56px: sidebar toggle, divider, breadcrumbs from `handle.crumb`, which holds the whole trail because

### T-R4.7 — As built

Migrations 00085 and 00086 add nullable profile fields and bounded object preferences. Self-scoped PATCH preserves omitted profile keys, clears only the documented nullable fields, and merges preference top-level keys with atomic audits. Private fields appear only in CurrentUser; public display names fall back to full names. Join preview exposes the same student-facing name field and no private profile data.

The frontend applies acknowledged account locale/theme/display zone/larger-test-text over browser mirrors, preserves mounted drafts, and keeps explicit Retry on failed preference writes. Same-page actor generations, serialized cookie transports and checked global draft/cache cleanup prevent old-account results from affecting a replacement account. Refresh remains single-flight. A 20-second admission/transport status bound and 10-second cleanup status bound do not abandon raw ownership; a timed-out admission cannot later sign in automatically. Cross-tab cookie ordering remains outside this task.

Display consumers react to the accepted zone. Existing teacher wall-clock inputs and their rules preview retain fixed-HCM interpretation until Task43; full redesigned profile controls remain43 and photo operations8. Compact-table and assignment-default consumers are not claimed implemented here. Anonymous preferences stay local and omitted defaults are read without materializing server writes. Unsupported browser zones preserve the stored value, report compatibility and temporarily display HCM without persisting that fallback.

Independent source reviews, actual PG18.6 app/migrate profile/preference tests with checked fixture cleanup and retained audits, unchanged high-risk canaries, contract generation/type/lint, hosted CI and the 12-family native cookie/IndexedDB/presentation campaign passed. The native suite used real browser cookie/IDB mechanics with controlled HTTP fixtures; server authority has separate Go/live CI coverage. Five-width/two-theme image acceptance includes the separately captured phone account menus. Earlier failures and repairs remain in the takeover evidence. This completes Task7 within R4, not a production release.

### T-R4.8 — Profile photo
**Depends on:** T-R4.7, T-R4.53
**Touches:** `server/internal/platform/imagesafe/` (new), `server/internal/modules/identity/{application/command/set_avatar.go (new),http/account.go}`, `server/internal/core/router/ratelimits.go`, `server/internal/core/wiring/identity.go`, an object-store port in `server/internal/modules/identity/application/ports`, `server/internal/core/router/tests/testdata/permissions.golden`, `api/openapi.yaml` (`IMAGE_DIMENSIONS` is a new `ErrorCode` value), generated code
**Size:** S
**As built (2026-10-10):**
- **Touched beyond the list:** `core/adapters/photos.go` (the identity port's implementation and the
  avatar limits), `identity/{domain,repositories/avatar.go,application/{app.go,ports,command/remove_avatar.go,
  query/avatar_url.go,internal/support/avatar.go},http/avatar.go}`, the pinned counts in
  `core/router/tests/{permissions_test.go (116 to 118 gated operations),me_test.go (6 to 8 under /me/),
  group_authoring_test.go (11 to 12 body budgets),principal_limits_test.go}`, and in `server/tests/` the two
  isolation entries, a 300 x 300 body for them and `profile_photo_flow_test.go`. **No migration:**
  `users.avatar_key` has existed since 00085.
- **The checks run cheapest first, and nothing is decoded until the sixth:** the request cap (2 MiB,
  413 before the handler), the multipart `file` part (400), the read through a `LimitReader` of the limit
  plus one byte (413 `MEDIA_TOO_LARGE`), the file signature (PNG or JPEG, 415 `MEDIA_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED`; the
  part's Content-Type is never read), `image.DecodeConfig` (415 `MEDIA_UNREADABLE`), the sides and the
  memory estimate (415 `IMAGE_DIMENSIONS`), then the slot, the decode, the crop, the resample and the encode.
- **One `imagesafe.Gate` of two slots serves the process**, built in `wiring/identity.go` and meant to be
  handed to R5's logo upload. The slot is taken after the file is read, so a slow uploader holds a
  connection and never a slot. A waiter leaves when its request context ends. **No "busy" answer and no new
  code were added** (decided): waiters stay bounded by the request context and the server's timeouts, each
  holding at most 2 MiB of encoded bytes.
- **`imagesafe.MaxDecodedBytes` is 48 MiB** (decided; 64 was refused: Argon2 can hold 256 MiB of a 512 MB
  machine). The estimate comes from the header: the decoded image by colour model, and for a progressive JPEG
  the `int32` coefficient block the standard decoder keeps per component (4 bytes a pixel per full-resolution
  component, read from `image/jpeg`). A progressive 4:4:4 JPEG at 2048 x 2048 needs 60 MiB, over about 1830
  pixels a side; a progressive CMYK one over about 1450; both answer 415 `IMAGE_DIMENSIONS` before any
  allocation. 16-bit PNG at 2048 x 2048 (32 MiB), baseline JPEG and 4:2:0 progressive are admitted. A test
  holds the estimate between 0.8 and 1.6 times what the decoders really allocate for seven kinds of image.
  The bound for two slots is 96 MiB of image memory.
- **The box filter is exact area averaging in integers** (centred square crop, any scale including a small
  image scaled up, premultiplied alpha, one pass), so a transparent pixel adds no colour and the result is
  the same on every machine. The output is always 256 x 256, encoded by the standard PNG encoder, which
  writes IHDR, IDAT and IEND only.
- **EXIF orientation is applied, then dropped** (decided). The reader takes the first Exif APP1 segment,
  reads IFD0 only and never follows a next-IFD offset, so no chain can loop; an IFD that declares more than
  256 entries or runs past the block, and any wrong byte order, magic, type, count or value, is ignored
  whole. The transform runs on the 256 x 256 result.
- **Both operations answer 200 `CurrentUser`**, and every `CurrentUser` (login, Google sign-in and link,
  `GET` and `PATCH /auth/me`) carries `avatarUrl`, a presigned GET valid 24 hours, when the user has a
  photo. **`User` (someone else) is unchanged:** a list that shows other people's photos (admin Users, the
  roster) needs a key and a signature per row and gets them in its own task. The signed URL's path names its
  object (`avatars/{userId}/{uuid}.png`), which is no secret from its owner.
- **The write is one statement:** a data-modifying CTE sets `avatar_key`, returns the old key and writes
  `user.avatar_set` or `user.avatar_removed` (the diff names no key). Concurrent writes chain: each deletes
  the key the other replaced. A failed write deletes the new object; a failed delete of the old one is
  logged and not returned. A crash between the put and the write, or a failed delete, can leave one
  unreferenced object that no response reaches; deleting a user does not delete the photo yet, and a sweep
  of the `avatars/` prefix is the fix for both (R5's anonymisation, T-R5.17a, should delete it).
- **Rate limit:** `PUT /me/avatar` is 10 an hour per user in `PrincipalRateLimits()`; `DELETE` is not
  limited. A deployment with no bucket answers `PUT` 501 and signs nothing; `DELETE` still clears the key.
- **The web half is T-R4.43.** A phone photo (4032 x 3024) is over 2048 on its long side and answers 415
  `IMAGE_DIMENSIONS`, so the client downscales on a canvas to at most 2048 on the long side before it
  uploads (F-45). The server's limits do not change.
**Done when:**
- [x] `PUT /me/avatar` (`self`, multipart, `x-max-body-bytes` 2 MiB): the type is sniffed (PNG
      or JPEG only, 415 otherwise); `image.DecodeConfig` runs before any decode and refuses under
      200×200 or over 2048×2048 (415 `IMAGE_DIMENSIONS`); decoding runs under a semaphore of two;
      the image is downscaled to 256×256 with an in-repo box filter and re-encoded as PNG, which
      drops EXIF; stored at `avatars/{userId}/{uuid}.png`; the previous object is deleted best
      effort
- [x] `DELETE /me/avatar` (`self`) clears it; `avatarUrl` is a presigned GET valid 24 hours
- [x] `platform/imagesafe` is reusable by R5's logo upload; no new dependency
- [x] Rate limit: 10 an hour per user in `PrincipalRateLimits()`, keyed by
      `ratelimit.PrincipalKey` (T-R4.53)
- [x] Tests: a small PNG declaring 30000×30000 is refused before decoding; EXIF is gone after
      upload; a GIF answers 415; replacing a photo removes the old object

- [ ] `DELETE /auth/sessions/{familyId}` (`self`) revokes that family, bumps the caller's
      session epoch and calls `Principals.Forget(userID)`; another user's family answers 404;
      the current family answers 409 (sign out instead). `POST /auth/sessions/revoke-others`
      (`self`) revokes every other family, bumps the epoch, calls `Principals.Forget(userID)`
      and returns `{revoked}`. The current tab's next request gets a 401 and recovers through
      the single-flight refresh
- [ ] Tests: rotation copies the label; revoked and expired families are not listed; a revoked
      device's access token fails on its next request on the same machine, and within R2's 10 s
      cache on any other; `client.refresh.test.ts` is green and untouched; another user's family
      answering 404 has a test of its own, because `/auth` is outside the isolation suite

### T-R4.10a — Notifications: store and read API
**Depends on:** —
**Touches:** `server/internal/modules/notifications/` (new: `domain`, `application/{command,query}`, `repositories`, `http`), `server/internal/core/wiring/notifications.go` (new), `server/internal/core/wiring/wiring.go`, `server/internal/core/router/{modules.go,server.go}`, `server/internal/core/router/router.go` (the `Server` literal), `server/internal/core/core.go`, `server/internal/core/jobs/prune.go`, `server/internal/platform/httpx/hardening.go` and `server/internal/core/router/tests/hardening_test.go`, `server/internal/core/router/tests/resource_contract_test.go`, `server/internal/core/router/tests/testdata/permissions.golden`, the isolation world and an entry for each of the five operations (mark-read excused and blind), `server/internal/modules/notifications/http/tests/language_test.go`, `web/tests/units/contract/openapi-contract.test.ts`, `migrations/00080_create_notifications.sql`, `migrations/00081_create_notification_preferences.sql`, `api/openapi.yaml`, generated code. `core/tests/messages_test.go` finds the new package by itself
**Size:** M
**As built:**
- **Touched beyond the list,** as T-R4.0 corrected it on the integration branch:
  `core/router/tests/permissions_test.go` (the count of gated operations, 97 to 102) and four new
  test files: `core/router/tests/me_test.go` (the tree through the router), `core/jobs/tests/prune_test.go`,
  `core/tests/jobs_test.go` (`Serve` starts both prunes; removing the line passed every test) and
  `server/tests/notifications_flow_test.go` (two users over HTTP, and the start-up path).
- **The contract is complete for the release.** Nine kinds, five events, one closed
  `NotificationParams`, a typed `NotificationTarget`; the kind table in `NotificationKind`'s
  description is checked against the Go type of each kind by a test. Operation ids:
  `listNotifications`, `markNotificationsRead`, `getMySummary`, `getNotificationPreferences`,
  `updateNotificationPreferences`, tag `me`. T-R4.10b and T-R4.22 add no contract line.
- **`Notify` is one statement.** `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (user_id, dedupe_key) DO UPDATE` takes the
  new kind and target, sets `read_at = NULL`, and under `domain.Add` sums `count` and `toGrade`
  with the stored values in SQL; `domain.Replace` stores the new params. `Merge` is a closed
  enum whose zero value is refused. A producer passes `command.Notify{UserID, Kind, Params,
  Target, DedupeKey, Merge}`; wiring builds the module first and holds it as `notificationsApp`,
  so a port is `notificationsApp.Commands.Notify`. The target of a merged row is the newest
  notice's, whatever the sums are: a paper with an answer to mark (target `grading`) followed in
  the same bucket by one without (target `assignment`) leaves `toGrade` 1 and the target
  `assignment`. So T-R4.10b's rule on the integration branch, "target Grading when `toGrade` is
  above zero", cannot be chosen from one paper's `toGrade`, and a target read from the
  assignment's state after the commit races as a count read first would. That task decides
  whether this statement or the web (from `params.toGrade`) applies the rule (decided: the web
  applies it, T-R4.45a).
- **The switch is applied inside that statement**, which the brief did not ask for and the plan
  needs: producers hold only the `Notify` port, so nothing else could apply it. The insert is
  skipped while the user's row for the kind's event has `in_app = false`. `domain.Kind.Event`
  maps the kinds: `assignment.opened` and `assignment.extended` fall under `assignment.due_soon`;
  `class.joined` and `join_codes.rotated` have no switch and are always written.
- **Params are a sealed set of Go types**, one per kind (`domain.Submitted`, `Flagged`, `Closing`,
  `Joined`, `CodesRotated`, `Opened`, `DueSoon`, `Extended`, `Ready`), encoded through one struct
  that has exactly the contract's names. `Notify` refuses params of another kind and values
  outside the contract's bounds; times are stored in UTC.
- **A target names what its route needs:** `assignment` and `studentAssignment` an `assignmentId`,
  `attempt` and `result` an `attemptId`; `grading` and `classes` need neither, and either id may
  accompany any route. Ids are canonical uuids.
- **Dedupe keys are free text of 1 to 200 characters.** The format producers are expected to use
  is `<what>:<id>[:<bucket>]`, for example `submitted:{assignmentId}:{unix seconds / 900}` and
  `flagged:{attemptId}`; nothing parses it.
- **The list is a keyset on the uuidv7 id.** `before` is declared `kind: none`: it bounds the
  caller's own page and looks nothing up. The page is 20 by default and 50 at most; the store
  asks for one row more to answer `nextBefore`.
- **Mark-read answers 204 whatever matched.** One command takes 1 to 100 ids, another marks all;
  an absent `ids` is the second, and an empty list is refused, so no id can never mean every row.
  `read_at` keeps its first value. No operation answers 403 or 404 for an id.
- **Preferences are all five switches for every role.** GET fills `inApp: true, email: false`
  for a switch never saved; PUT takes each event exactly once in any order (the schema refuses
  an unknown event and any count but five, the handler a repeated one) and answers them in the
  enum's order.
- **`updated_at` moves by the trigger** on both tables (spec §13.2), so a mark-read moves it too;
  `created_at`, the id and the place in the list never move on a merge.
- **Retention:** `jobs.PruneNotifications` runs at start-up and daily under its own one-minute
  budget and deletes rows first written more than 180 days ago, strictly.
- **The isolation suite's case for mark-read is excused and blind**, as planned, so the proof
  that one user cannot read or mark another's is elsewhere: the two-user integration tests in
  `notifications/repositories/tests` and the HTTP flow in `server/tests/notifications_flow_test.go`.
  The suite's snapshot of the other teacher now includes their notifications.
- **The migrations are named `NNNNN_…` and must be numbered before CI:** goose refuses a file
  whose prefix is not a number. Neither depends on the other.
**Done when:**
- [x] `create_notifications`: `id uuid PK DEFAULT uuidv7()`, `user_id` FK users `ON DELETE
      CASCADE`, `kind text` (dotted key, ≤64), `params jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'` (object, ≤4 KB),
      `target jsonb` (object, ≤1 KB), `dedupe_key text NOT NULL` (≤200), `created_at`,
      `updated_at`, `read_at`; `UNIQUE (user_id, dedupe_key)`; indexes `(user_id, id DESC)`,
      `(user_id) WHERE read_at IS NULL` and `(created_at)`. The app role gets SELECT, INSERT,
      UPDATE and DELETE by migration 00009's default privileges; no statement. Down drops the
      table
- [x] `create_notification_preferences`: `(user_id FK CASCADE, event text, in_app boolean NOT
      NULL, email boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false, updated_at, PRIMARY KEY (user_id, event))`
      with `CONSTRAINT notification_preferences_event_check CHECK (event IN
      ('attempt.submitted','attempt.flagged','assignment.closing','assignment.due_soon',
      'result.ready'))`, one key per switch of T-R4.44 and T-R4.45 (`assignment.due_soon` is the
      student's "Test due soon", which also governs `assignment.opened` and
      `assignment.extended`). R7 and R10 replace this constraint by name. A missing row means the
      deck's default. `email` exists now so R7 adds no column. Down drops the table
- [x] Contract, all `x-permission: self`: `GET /me/notifications?before=&limit=` →
      `{items: [{id, kind, params, target, createdAt, readAt}], nextBefore}`; `POST
      /me/notifications/read {ids?}` (≤100; absent means all); `GET /me/summary` →
      `{unreadNotifications}`; `GET` and `PUT /me/notification-preferences` → `[{event, inApp,
      email}]`. `NotificationKind` and the event keys are enums; `target` is a typed route
      (`assignment`, `attempt`, `grading`, `classes`, `result`, `studentAssignment`)
- [x] `SecurityHeaders` adds `/me/` to its `Cache-Control: no-store` prefixes;
      `TestSecurityHeadersOnSuccessFailureAndPreflight` asserts no-store on one path per private
      tree (`/auth`, `/app`, `/teacher`, `/admin`, `/me`) and that `/public/status` keeps its own
      public `max-age`
- [x] Text is rendered by the web from `kind` and `params` in the reader's locale; `params` are
      a Go struct per kind, never markup. A name in `params` is a copy: R5's anonymisation
      (T-R5.17a) deletes or scrubs notifications that name an anonymised user
- [x] `Notify` upserts on `(user_id, dedupe_key)` and can merge counts into `params`; a merge of
      counts is computed inside the one `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` statement, never from a
      row read first; an integration test of N concurrent calls on one dedupe key pins it
- [x] The daily prune in `core/jobs/prune.go` deletes notifications older than 180 days
- [x] The student-payload walk (R3's leak test) covers `/me/notifications`; the operations join
      the isolation suite's two-user `/me/*` pair (user A cannot read or mark user B's): the
      `x-resource` enum gains `notification`, and `POST /me/notifications/read` declares it for
      its `ids` body field
- [x] Tests: unit (upsert merge), integration (cursor paging, mark one and all, cap of 100 ids,
      preferences default), contract; `20-data-model.md` records both tables and a new
      deviation row for `params jsonb`

### T-R4.10b — Notifications: producers and due-time items
**Depends on:** T-R4.10a, T-R4.11, T-R4.12
**Touches:** `server/internal/modules/{attempts,assignments,classes}/application/ports/` (new `Notifier` ports), `server/internal/modules/attempts/application/{command/submit.go,command/finish.go,internal/support/integrity.go}`, `server/internal/modules/classes/application/command/enrol_new_member.go`, `server/internal/modules/classes/application/command/enrol_existing.go` (the signed-in student's join), `server/internal/modules/notifications/{application/command/materialise_due.go (new),repositories/due.go (new)}`, `server/internal/core/wiring/*.go`, `web/src/features/assignments/pages/AssignmentIntroPage.tsx`, `web/src/lib/i18n/locales/`, `web/tests/units/student/intro.test.tsx`
**Size:** M
**As built (2026-10-10):**
- **No migration, no contract line.** The unique key `(user_id, dedupe_key)` and the indexes
  `class_members_user_idx`, `assignment_classes_class_idx` and `assignment_students_user_idx`
  serve everything below; `00102` is not claimed. The contract changed in prose only: the
  descriptions of `extendAssignment`, `setStudentOverrides`, `listNotifications` and `getMySummary`
  say what `notify` and the due-time write now do.
- **Telling after commit is one helper.** `shared/cqrs.Announce` runs a `build` that reads what a
  notice says and returns the `Notify` commands, then delivers them in order under a ten-second
  budget on a context that keeps the request's values and not its end. A failure is logged once with
  the count undelivered and never returned; a nil handler reads nothing. The `Notifier` ports in
  attempts, assignments and classes are the notifications `Notify` handler, as classes' already was.
  `WithNotifier` is the only wiring (`core/wiring`), so an application built without one tells
  nobody and every existing test is unchanged.
- **Who hears of a paper.** `visibility.PaperReaders` is `visibility.Papers` read the other way: the
  assignment's creator, and the teacher of a target class when the student is a member of a class
  they teach, an account they created, or a named target of an assignment they created. The plan's
  "the teachers of its target classes" is read as the teachers of the target classes *the student is
  in*: otherwise a teacher of class Y would be told a paper they cannot open. A test holds
  `PaperReaders` equal to `Papers` over a world with every route to a student. Disabled teachers
  are not written to.
- **Attempts return what a write brought about.** `Submit`, `Save`, `Flush` and `ExpireIfDue` return
  `domain.Milestones{AttemptID, HandedIn, Flagged}`; `Finish` is announced by its command with
  `Graded`. `deriveFocusLoss` reports the false-to-true move of `flagged` with `RETURNING NOT
  old.flagged AND new.flagged`, so the integrity policy flags a paper once and a later absence on a
  flagged paper tells nobody; the limit's own `UPDATE flagged = true` reports the same way. A
  teacher's flag and a `warn` policy never reach the code at all. A paper closed by time (`timed_out`)
  is a hand-in. The command then asks `Briefing(attemptID, now)` for the title, the student's name, the
  focus-loss count, whether an answer still waits for a mark (`toGrade` is 0 or 1 per paper, summed
  by `domain.Add` in SQL), whether the review policy shows the score under the student's own close,
  and the readers. A voided or missing attempt answers `ErrNotFound` and nothing is sent.
- **`attempt.submitted`** is keyed `submitted:{assignmentId}:{unix / 900}` with `Add`; its target is
  the assignment (the web sends a positive `toGrade` to Grading, T-R4.45a). **`attempt.flagged`** is
  keyed `flagged:{attemptId}` with `Replace` and targets the attempt. **`class.joined`** is keyed
  `joined:{classId}:{studentId}`: `EnrolResult` gains `StudentName` and `TeacherID`, read in the
  enrolment's transaction, and both `EnrolExisting` and `EnrolNewMember` tell once it commits, not for
  a repeat join. `classes.teacher_id` is `NOT NULL`, so a class always has a teacher to tell.
- **`result.ready` on finish** is sent when `Finish` succeeds and `review_show_score AND
  (release = 'on_submit' OR now >= the student's close)`; a refused finish sends nothing and a second
  finish sends the same key again (the store keeps one row, unread again). The row holds the title; the
  target holds the attempt and the assignment; no score or band is read into it, and a test checks the
  stored JSON.
- **`assignment.extended`.** `extendAssignment` with `notify` tells every enabled, student-like student
  of a published assignment whose own close the extension changed: those without an override and those
  whose override closes before the new close (an override at or after it keeps its close and hears
  nothing). `setStudentOverrides` with `notify` tells the students named when the request sets
  `extendBy` or `closesAt` and their override now closes after the assignment's own; attempts and
  time limit move no close and tell nobody. The key is `extended:{assignmentId}` with `Replace`, so the
  latest close replaces the earlier notice. The recipients are read after the commit, so a student is
  told the close in force then.
- **`MaterialiseDue(userID, now)`** runs first in `GET /me/summary` and `GET /me/notifications`, behind
  an in-process throttle of five minutes per user (a failed run forgets its slot; a map above 4,096
  entries drops the expired). It reads the due notices in one statement (`Postgres.Due`) and writes
  them in one (`InsertAbsent`, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` under the same switch `Upsert` applies), so a
  row the user already holds stays as it is, read or not. A failure is logged by the transport and does
  not fail the read. Moments lie in (now − 7 days, now]:
  - *opened*: `greatest(opens_at, published_at)`, while the student's close is ahead;
  - *due soon*: the student's close − 24 h and − 1 h (`schedule.CloseOf` with their override and the
    early close), the day's only while the hour's moment is still ahead, so a first read inside the last
    hour yields the hour's alone (decided at review, Q1), while the close is ahead, they have no hand-in (a submitted, timed-out or graded
    attempt, or one left in progress past its deadline) and have an attempt left (non-voided attempts
    below `max_attempts` plus their extra attempts), and the test was available at that moment;
  - *closing* (teachers): the assignment's close − 1 h, with the number of enabled student-like members
    of its roster that `visibility.Papers` shows the user, who have no hand-in and whose own close is
    the assignment's (an override that reaches later leaves a student out); nothing when the count is 0;
  - *ready* (students): the student's close for an `after_close` release that shows the score, for each
    handed-in attempt with no answer waiting for a mark.
  The keys of the reminders and of the closing notice name the close (`DueSoonKey`, `ClosingKey`), so a
  close that moves earns them again. A student is a target only if enabled and in
  `app.student_like_roles`. The read skips an assignment whose close is before the lookback, since no
  kind can still be earned from it; on 12,100 users, 3,000 assignments and 45,000 attempts a student's
  read runs in about 1.1 ms and touches about 150 buffers, a teacher's in about 1.2 ms (EXPLAIN at the
  size production will reach is T-R4.52's).
- **The Test intro** reads the "Test due soon" switch through `useDueSoonOn`, only in the scheduled
  state; the line stays T-R3.8's while the switches are unread, unreadable or off.
- **Decisions the plan did not make** (build the default; each is one line to change):
  - `result.ready` at a close is sent for a paper that needs no marking, even if the teacher never
    "finishes" it; a paper waiting for marks is announced by `Finish` instead;
  - `attempt.submitted` counts a timed-out paper, and a paper closed by the focus limit, as a hand-in;
  - the audience of a paper is read after the commit, so a teacher who stopped teaching a class between
    the two is not told.
**Done when:**
- [x] Each producing module declares a `Notifier` port typed as the notifications `Notify`
      handler (AGENTS: a cross-module need is a port typed as the other module's handler); calls
      run after the producing transaction commits, and a failure is logged, never returned
- [x] Teacher recipients are the assignment's creator and the teachers of its target classes,
      deduplicated, each subject to their preferences:
  - `attempt.submitted` (default on): dedupe `submitted:{assignmentId}:{15-minute bucket}`,
    params `{title, count, toGrade}`, target the assignment; the web sends a positive
    `toGrade` to Grading for a reader who can grade (T-R4.45a, "As built")
  - `attempt.flagged` (default on): when the integrity policy flags an attempt, dedupe
    `flagged:{attemptId}`, params `{studentName, title, focusLost}`, target the attempt sheet
  - `assignment.closing` (default on): a due-time item at close − 1 h while someone has not
    handed in, params `{title, notSubmitted}`
  - `class.joined` (no switch; the deck draws none): on a join by code
- [x] Student recipients:
  - `assignment.opened`, `assignment.due_soon` (close − 24 h and − 1 h, while the student has
    not handed in and has attempts left) and `assignment.extended` (T-R4.12 with notify on),
    all under the "Test due soon" switch
  - `result.ready` under "Result ready": on finish grading when the review policy shows the
    score now, or as a due-time item at close for `after_close`. It carries the attempt id
    and the title, never a score or band: the web shows the score from the result query, which
    applies the policy in force when it is read
- [x] `MaterialiseDue(userID, now)` runs inside `GET /me/summary` and `GET /me/notifications`,
      at most once per five minutes per user (in-process map); it inserts items whose trigger
      time lies in (now − 7 days, now] with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`; there is no timer and no
      background job
- [x] Effective close times include per-student overrides (T-R4.12) and early closes
- [x] The Test intro's line under "Opens …" (`student.intro.opensHint`) becomes the deck's "We
      will notify you when it opens." for a student whose "Test due soon" switch is on, now
      that `assignment.opened` exists; with the switch off it stays T-R3.8's "You can start
      once it opens." (`web/src/features/assignments/pages/AssignmentIntroPage.tsx`, locales,
      `web/tests/units/student/intro.test.tsx`)
- [x] Tests: every producer (unit and integration); materialisation boundaries (exactly
      T − 24 h, closed early, extended, an override for one student); preferences off writes
      nothing; a teacher of class X receives nothing about class Y; no `result.ready` row holds
      a score
- [x] `attempts/application/tests/events_test.go` is green and untouched; attempts unit tests run before and after
      (high-risk area)

### T-R4.11 — Assignment review options, the note to students and the live lock
**Depends on:** —
**Touches:** `migrations/NNNNN_add_assignment_review_options.sql`, `migrations/NNNNN_add_assignments_student_note.sql`, `api/openapi.yaml` (`ReviewPolicy`, `Assignment`, `AssignmentInput`, `StudentAssignmentDetail`, `ErrorCode`, the student result schema), generated code, `server/internal/modules/assignments/{domain/assignment.go,application/command/update.go,repositories/write.go,repositories/student.go}`, `server/internal/modules/attempts/{application/query/result.go,repositories/result.go}`, the R3 student result page in `web/src/features/results/`, `web/src/features/assignments/pages/AssignmentIntroPage.tsx` (the note block), `web/tests/units/student/intro.test.tsx`
**Size:** M
**As built (2026-10-08):**
- **The migrations are `00099` and `00100`,** the next numbers after `00098` on
  `work/redesign-r4` at `9ca8d64f`. They were `00093` and `00094` when cut from `d6c5f8cc` and
  were renumbered when T-R4.62a landed `00097` and `00098` (T-5); renumber them again if another
  file lands first. `00099` adds
  `review_release text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'on_submit'` with the named check
  `assignments_review_release_check` and `review_show_class_average boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT
  false`; `00100` adds the nullable `student_note` with `assignments_student_note_check`
  (`char_length(btrim(student_note, <the 25 whitespace code points of shared/answered>)) BETWEEN 1
  AND 500`, QA-11-2: `btrim` alone strips only the space, so a note of a tab or a newline passed
  the check while the command dropped it), added with the column. `StudentNoteOf` trims that
  same set, so the check, the answered rule and the Go trim agree; a test pins the three. Every
  default is
  a constant, so nothing is rewritten and the previous release's insert keeps its meaning. D-34
  and D-35 record them (D-33 is T-R4.62a's).
- **Contract.** `ReviewRelease` (`on_submit`, `after_close`); `ReviewPolicy` (responses) requires
  `release` and `showClassAverage`, and `ReviewPolicyInput` (`AssignmentInput.review`) makes both
  optional; `AssignmentInput.maxAttempts` has `maximum: 99`; `studentNote` is optional and
  nullable on `AssignmentInput` and on `StudentAssignmentDetail`, and required and nullable on
  `Assignment`; the result gains optional `releasesAt` and `classAverage` (0 to 100); `ErrorCode`
  gains `ASSIGNMENT_LOCKED`, the 409 of `updateAssignment`. No operation was added, so the
  permission golden, the operation count and the isolation table are unchanged.
- **The update is partial for the three new fields.** `release`, `showClassAverage` and
  `studentNote` are `json.RawMessage` or optional on the wire; `WriteInput` carries
  `ReleaseSet`, `ClassAverageSet` and `StudentNoteSet`, and the `UPDATE` reads
  `CASE WHEN $set THEN $value ELSE column END`. The SPA's full-replace body therefore keeps the
  stored values, and `studentNote: null` clears the note. A create takes the defaults. The 500
  characters are counted before trimming at the contract and after it in `WriteInput.Validate`;
  the command stores NULL for a note that is blank once trimmed of the same whitespace.
- **The lock** is `requireUnlocked`, called right after `lockForUpdate`, before
  `publishedTestFor` and `versionStillFree`. While the stored row's derived status at `in.Now` is
  `open`, a change to `testVersionId`, `durationMinutes` or `maxAttempts` answers
  `ASSIGNMENT_LOCKED`, with or without attempts, whichever version it names; sending the stored
  value is not a change. A scheduled or closed assignment with attempts still answers
  `VERSION_LOCKED`, and its timing stays editable. The status flips exactly at `opens_at` (open)
  and `closes_at` (closed), and `TestTheLockFlipsExactlyAtTheEdgesOfTheWindow` pins both.
  Two existing tests re-pointed an open assignment; they now do it on a scheduled one.
- **`after_close`** reads, in `LoadResult(ctx, attempt, now)`, the stored policy and the close.
  Until `now` reaches it, the effective flags are all false, so the existing gates select no
  score, mark, key or explanation, and the grader's comment is not selected either (default for
  Thuong). `releasesAt` carries the close; `review.release` and `review.showClassAverage` are
  echoed as stored. The student card's score flag is `review_show_score AND (review_release =
  'on_submit' OR now() >= close)`; the intro's detail carries the stored flags, the release and
  the note. Pending manual answers and transcripts keep their own gates.
- **The close is `least(closed_at, closes_at)`,** not `coalesce(closed_at, closes_at)` as the
  approach wrote it: closing an assignment already past its window sets `closed_at` after
  `closes_at`, and `DerivedStatus` calls that closed at `closes_at`. `shared/schedule` gains
  `CloseOf(override)` (SQL), `Close` (Go), `Override` and `Window.WithOverride`; T-R4.11 passes no
  override and T-R4.12 fills it.
- **`classAverage`** is the mean over targeted, enabled students (`assignment_classes` through
  `class_members`, or `assignment_students`, the roster `rulesQuery` uses) of each student's
  best `score_earned / score_total` among `graded` attempts, as a percent rounded to two
  decimals. `voided`, `submitted`, `timed_out` and not-started students count nothing; a
  student who left the class or was disabled counts nothing. It is absent below
  `ClassAverageFloor` (3) qualifying students, while the reader's result is withheld, before the
  assignment's close and with the switch off. The page prints at most one decimal.
- **`LoadResult` names two closes,** `readerClose` and `assignmentClose`, both
  `schedule.Close(closesAt, closedAt, nil)` today. `Withheld` and `releasesAt` read
  `readerClose`; `classAverageOf` is told whether `assignmentClose` has passed. T-R4.12 passes a
  student's override to `readerClose` alone (R11-2).
- **The class average's plan, measured on a seeded scratch database** (`EXPLAIN (ANALYZE,
  BUFFERS)` of `classAverageOf`'s statement for an assignment that targets three classes and five
  named students, 125 students, 194 graded attempts; the database was dropped afterwards). No
  index was added.
  - 3,000 students in 75 classes of 40, 300 assignments, 24,250 attempts (about 8 per student):
    the planner reads each of the 125 roster students through `attempts_student_started_idx`
    and filters the assignment and status. 2.7 ms, 1,604 buffers, all shared hits.
  - The same with 3,000 assignments and 240,250 attempts (89 MB): `attempts_assignment_status_idx
    (assignment_id, status)` is the access path, one search that returns the 194 rows from 10
    buffers. 1.8 ms, 634 buffers.

    ```
    Aggregate                                        actual time=1.5  Buffers: shared hit=634
      GroupAggregate on at.student_id                rows=122
        Hash Join (at.student_id = m.user_id)        rows=194
          Index Scan using attempts_assignment_status_idx on attempts
                                                     rows=194  Index Searches: 1  shared hit=10
          HashAggregate over the roster              rows=125  shared hit=39
            Hash Join: Bitmap Index Scan on assignment_classes_pkey x Seq Scan on class_members (shared hit=35)
            Seq Scan on assignment_students          shared hit=1
        Index Scan using users_pkey                  rows=1  loops=194
    Execution Time: 1.765 ms
    ```
  - `class_members` is read by a sequential scan, because 3,000 rows are 35 buffers. With
    sequential scans disabled the roster goes through `assignment_classes_pkey` and
    `class_members_pkey` (`class_id` leads) with a Bitmap Index Scan of 40 rows per class, and the
    statement runs in 1.0 ms, so the primary key is the path once the table outgrows a page scan.
    That one is a diagnostic and not what the planner picks at this size.
- **Web.** The intro draws the teacher's note between "Before you start" and the action, as the
  Student deck does (initials circle, "Note from {name}", the text), only when present, as
  plain text with its line breaks. The score sentence follows `release` ("after the test
  closes"), which T-R4.27a had listed; its preview shares `studentRules.ts`, and the rules
  input takes a release that may be absent. The result page says when a withheld result is
  released, in the lock line and the summary, and draws one muted line under its sentence,
  "Class average: 62%." The Student deck draws no class-average line; the Tech Lead was told.
  Fourteen test files that carry a review policy were swept in their own commit.
- **Review REV-11 (APPROVE WITH NITS)** found no student leak, withholding at selection time, the
  lock under the row lock, and DDL, scoping and isolation unchanged. Its fixes: R11-2 above;
  R11-3, the plan above; R11-4, `StudentAssignmentDetail.review` says in the contract that the
  intro's flags are the stored ones and take effect only on the result; R11-1, the floor of 3,
  is recorded as an exposure in DG-137 and listed for Thuong's sign-off; R11-5, the Downs stay.
- **Verification VER-11 (PASS WITH FINDINGS)** found withholding, the class average, the lock, the
  note, the web at 360 and 1280 in vi and en, the migrations and three mutants sound. Fixed here:
  QA-11-2, above (the check's whitespace); QA-11-3, `clockTime` takes the locale and writes the day
  as "12 Oct" in English, so the withheld result's "at 02:34, 12/10" no longer reads as
  December 10th (Vietnamese keeps "12/10"). Logged outside this task: QA-11-1, a NUL in any
  free-text field answers 500, which predates it; O1, the average shows with `showScore` off
  (T-R4.27a's brief); O2, the 500 characters are counted before the trim, which the contract says.
- **Not built here:** the teacher's field for the note and the release switches (DG-71, T-R4.27a
  and the Settings tab), and the extension of the close by an override (T-R4.12).
**Done when:**
- [x] `add_assignment_review_options`: `review_release text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'on_submit' CHECK
