### T-R4.12 — Extensions and per-student overrides
**Depends on:** —
**Touches:** `migrations/00101_create_assignment_student_overrides.sql`, `api/openapi.yaml`, generated code, `server/internal/shared/schedule/close.go`, `server/internal/modules/assignments/{domain/{overrides,errors,repository}.go,application/{app.go,command/{extend,set_overrides,delete_override}.go,query/overrides.go},repositories/{overrides,student}.go,http/overrides.go}`, `server/internal/modules/attempts/{domain/repository.go,application/query/get.go,repositories/{store,result,review_review}.go}` (12a); `server/internal/shared/schedule/deadlines.go` (new), `server/internal/modules/assignments/{domain/overrides.go,repositories/{overrides,reopen,student,write}.go}`, `server/internal/modules/attempts/{domain/{attempt,commands,monitor,repository}.go,application/internal/support/service.go,http/admin_attempts.go,repositories/{store,monitor}.go}`, `server/internal/core/maintenance/windows.go` (12b); `server/tests/{join_and_take_flow,isolation_cases,isolation_world}_test.go`, `server/internal/core/router/tests/{permissions,resource_contract}_test.go` and `testdata/permissions.golden`, `docs/quizzivy-spec-v0.3.md`, `docs/plan/{20-data-model,60-backend-architecture}.md`
**Size:** L, shipped as two pull requests (decided when it started, 2026-10-10).
**Split:**
- **12a** (`feature/t-r4-12a-overrides`, migration `00101`): the table, `extendAssignment`,
  `setStudentOverrides`, `listStudentOverrides` and `deleteStudentOverride`; the student's card and
  intro, `startOrResumeAttempt` and the result's reader close and attempt count under the student's
  override; the isolation entries, the permission golden and the `join_and_take_flow_test.go` case.
  Nothing in 12a writes `app.attempts`, so an attempt already in progress keeps the deadline it has
  until 12b.
- **12b:** the recompute of in-progress deadlines under the assignment's lock and each attempt's row
  lock, the monitor's "Extended to …" and R1's maintenance extension over override close times. It
  is the part that writes `attempts` while a student is sitting, so it is reviewed on its own, with
  the concurrency tests the Senior Tester named.

Each "Done when" line below carries the pull request that closes it.
**As built (12a, 2026-10-10):**
- **The migration is `00101`,** the next number after `00100` on `work/redesign-r4` (T-R4.11's last).
  D-36 and `20-data-model.md` §38 record it. The four checks are named
  `assignment_student_overrides_{duration,extra_attempts,reason,changes}_check`, and the file adds
  `assignment_student_overrides_student_idx` and the `updated_at` trigger. The reason check reads the
  length of the trimmed reason the command stores, so a reason of only whitespace is refused by the
  command and never reaches it.
- **Contract.** Four operations: `extendAssignment` (`POST …/extend`, `teaching.assignments.write`),
  and `listStudentOverrides`, `setStudentOverrides` and `deleteStudentOverride`
  (`teaching.attempts.intervene`). `ErrorCode` gains `ASSIGNMENT_CLOSED`; the `x-resource` kinds gain
  `override`, the id of the student an override is on, read beside the assignment in the path. The
  list is unpaged (`{items}`) and declares `x-resource-list: override`, which makes 14 teacher lists.
  The permission golden gains the four, the operation pin goes from 109 to 113, and the Assistant,
  who holds `teaching.assignments.write` and not `teaching.attempts.intervene`, is refused 38.
  `StudentAssignmentCard` and `StudentAssignmentDetail` say their window is the student's own.
- **Decisions that were open questions.**
  - *Merge or replace:* a field the request names replaces the stored one and one it leaves out is
    kept, so a longer time limit does not take away a student's added attempts. The reason always
    replaces, and the audit log keeps every earlier one. No field is cleared singly: `extraAttempts:
    0` clears the attempts, and `deleteStudentOverride` takes the whole row. A request that would leave
    a row changing nothing is a 400 on `extraAttempts`.
  - *`extendBy`:* minutes (1–10080) added to the student's current close, which is the later of their
    override's and the assignment's own. It is refused with 409 `ASSIGNMENT_CLOSED` for a student
    whose close has passed, because that is a reopening and `closesAt` does it. `extendBy` and
    `closesAt` together are a 400, and `closesAt` must be ahead.
  - *Early close:* the effective close is `greatest(least(closed_at, closes_at), override.closes_at)`
    (T-R4.11). An override that reaches past the assignment's close lifts its early close for that
    student alone, and closing early later does not remove an override; `deleteStudentOverride`
    does.
  - *`extend` on a draft or a scheduled assignment* moves its `closes_at` like an open one. Only
    `closed`, by the same database-clock rule `reopenAssignment` uses, answers 409; the two operations
    take complementary sets.
  - *Reach:* every id in `studentIds` must be an enabled student-like target of the assignment (a
    member of a target class or named on it) whom the caller reaches (`visibility.StudentIDs`, or
    anyone under `scope.all`). One that is not answers 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` for the whole request,
    `details.studentIds` naming the ids, and nothing is written. A missing student, a teacher's
    student and a disabled one answer alike. The list and the delete read only the students the caller
    reaches, as the monitor does.
  - *`notify`* is recorded in the audit entry of `extend` and of each override. T-R4.10b is its
    consumer: it tells the students (see that task's "As built").
  - *The monitor's contract field for "Extended to …"* and the maintenance extension are 12b's.
- **Reads of one student's window.** `shared/schedule` gains `OverrideJoin`, `OverrideSelect` and
  `OverrideColumns`; `Window` carries `ClosedAt`, and `WithOverride` lifts it when the override reaches
  past it. `attempts.Rules` and `RulesFor` (now `RulesFor(assignmentID, studentID)`) return the
  student's window, time limit and attempts, so a start, a resume, `Deadline` and the attempt limit
  follow them without a change to the service. `LoadResult` passes the override's close to
  `readerClose` and its added attempts to `maxAttempts`, and `assignmentClose` stays the assignment's
  own (R11-2). The teacher's review of a paper counts the added attempts too, so "attempt 2 of 1"
  cannot be drawn. `ForStudent` and `StudentDetail` apply the override at the scan, and the home order
  follows the student's own close. The class and dashboard counts stay the assignment's.
- **Concurrency.** `setStudentOverrides` holds the assignment row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, which
  serialises writers of one assignment and does not block an attempt's insert. The upsert inserts the
  merged row (§38), because PostgreSQL checks `CHECK`s on the proposed row before it finds the
  conflict.
- **Deadlines in progress.** Nothing here writes `attempts`: an attempt running when a teacher extends
  keeps its deadline until 12b. `extendAssignment`'s description said so; 12b removes the sentence.
**As built (12b, 2026-10-10):**
- **No migration, one contract field.** `MonitorRow.extendedTo` (nullable `date-time`, optional) is the
  only schema change; the descriptions of `extendAssignment`, `setStudentOverrides` and
  `deleteStudentOverride` now say what they do to an attempt in progress, and `closesAt` is "ahead of
  the database's clock". The operation, permission and isolation pins do not move: no operation is new.
- **Every writer of a window recomputes (R12b-1, found in review).** The plan named `extendAssignment`
  and `setStudentOverrides`, but `updateAssignment` leaves `closesAt` editable on an open assignment
  (the live lock covers only the version, the duration and the attempts) and `reopenAssignment` lifts
  an early close and sets a later one, so each also takes `schedule.LockWindows` before the assignment
  row (for `Update`, before `lockForUpdate`) and calls `RecomputeDeadlines` for the whole assignment
  after its write, with the causes `assignment_updated` and `assignment_reopened`. The recompute only
  lengthens, so it runs unconditionally: a shorter close, or Close now through `Update`, moves nothing.
  `window-schedule` is the only other writer of `closes_at`, `closed_at` or an override.
  `TestEveryWriterOfAnAssignmentsWindowWaitsForTheMaintenanceLock` holds `(73819, 40)` exclusively
  from a second connection and shows each of the five writers wait for it by its application name in
  `pg_stat_activity`; `TestEveryWriterOfAWindowTakesTheSameLock` still pins that the three packages
  name one key. Mutants: no recompute in `Update`, none in `Reopen`, a recompute that also shortens,
  and no lock in `Reopen` each fail the test for it.
- **The recompute is in the kernel,** `shared/schedule.RecomputeDeadlines`, because assignments writes
  the window and the rows it moves are attempts'. One statement: a `live` CTE selects the assignment's
  attempts with `status = 'in_progress'` (all of them, or the named students'), `ORDER BY id FOR UPDATE
  OF at`, with `least(started_at + coalesce(o.duration_minutes, a.duration_minutes), CloseOf("o"))`;
  `moved` updates only where that is later than `deadline_at`; `logged` inserts one `attempt.extended`
  audit entry per attempt moved (`deadline_at` old and new, the cause, the assignment) from `moved`'s
  `RETURNING old.…, new.…`. It uses `schedule.CloseOf("o")`, never `a.closes_at` alone (R12a-6), so a
  student whose override closes later keeps it, and a deadline a maintenance window pushed past the
  rule stays. `status = 'in_progress'` is the filter, not `submitted_at IS NULL`: an attempt handed in
  while the statement waited for its row lock is skipped. **An attempt whose deadline has passed is
  over, swept or not (R12b-2):** `live` also requires `deadline_at > now()`, on the database's clock
  as R12a-3 settled, so no writer of a window revives one, and the outcome never depends on whether
  the sweeper or the student's client got to it first (autosave already answers such an attempt
  `ErrDeadlinePassed`). `TestAnAttemptPastItsDeadlineIsNotRevivedByAnyWriterOfTheWindow` puts an
  in-progress attempt a minute past its deadline beside one still ahead and runs `Extend`, `Update`,
  `SetOverrides` and `Reopen`; without the filter all four move it. `Extend` runs it for everyone and
  `SetOverrides` for the named students. `DeleteOverride` takes the same locks and does not run it:
  removing an override can only shorten a student's window, and a deadline never shortens, so a
  running attempt is left where it is.
- **Lock order, always:** the maintenance-windows advisory lock `(73819, 40)` shared, the assignment
  row, then the attempt rows in id order (`20-data-model.md` §28, §38). The five writers take the
  advisory lock first (`schedule.LockWindows`) and the assignment row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` (the
  update keeps the `FOR UPDATE` it always took); an attempt start takes the same two, the row
  `FOR SHARE`. The advisory lock is the reason: `window-schedule`
  takes it exclusively and then updates assignments and attempts in one statement, so a writer that
  held the assignment row and then asked for the lock could wait on a schedule that waits on the row.
  `TestEveryWriterOfAWindowTakesTheSameLock` pins that the maintenance, availability and schedule
  packages name one key.
- **R12a-5, the start race.** `attempts.Store.Create` no longer trusts the rules the service read
  before its transaction. The maintenance guard goes first, as before; the transaction then reads the
  student's rules again (`rulesQuery … FOR SHARE OF a`), refuses a student who is not targeted
  (`ErrForbidden`) or a window that is not open (`Rules.CanStartAt`, the service's `CanStart` rule),
  and stores `deadline_at` and the test version from that read. The service's `Deadline` only tells the
  first guard how far to look; when the in-transaction deadline is later, the guard runs again over the
  longer span. `Create` returns the rules it committed under and the session is built from them. A
  start that read before an extension committed therefore either waits for it on the share lock and
  reads the new close, or inserts first, and the extension's recompute finds the attempt. `FOR SHARE OF
  o` is not taken: PostgreSQL refuses a row lock on the nullable side of an outer join, and the override
  is only written under the assignment lock the start already holds `FOR SHARE`.
- **Evidence for the race.** `start_window_race_test.go`: a pausable guard stops a start after it passed
  the guard, an extension commits, the start resumes, and its deadline is the extended one
  (deterministic); a test-owned transaction holds `FOR SHARE` and inserts an attempt while a goroutine's
  `Extend` blocks on `assignment.extended` until it commits, then the attempt carries the extended
  deadline; two 16-round loops run start with extend, and extend with resume and submit of one attempt,
  neither deadlocking nor shortening. Two mutants were run and each fails its test: storing the
  caller's deadline in `Create` fails the deterministic test ("it was cut off by the close it read
  before"), and a recompute that names nobody on `Extend` fails the blocked-extension test ("the
  attempt inserted before the extension committed has deadline … want …").
- **R12a-3.** The "closes ahead" check is `SELECT $1::timestamptz > now()` in the transaction, so the
  database's clock judges it; `Overrides.Validate` no longer compares to `Now`, which is the audit
  stamp. **R12a-4.** `student.go` orders the home list by `schedule.CloseOf("o")`.
- **R12a-7, decision 3 kept.** An override survives a later early close: closing early never touches
  `assignment_student_overrides`, and the student's effective close is still `greatest(least(closed_at,
  closes_at), o.closes_at)`. `recompute_test.go` pins that a close later than the override's does not
  take it back. The "Close early" dialog's obligation is in T-R4.26 below.
- **The monitor.** `MonitorRow.extendedTo` is the override's `closes_at` when it is later than the
  assignment's own effective close (the earlier of its early close and its `closes_at`), else absent,
  so a student whose override closes sooner than the assignment reads nothing. For an assignment
  closed early it is the time a student kept open by an override has until, which is how the monitor
  marks them as still open (R12a-7).
- **The maintenance extension.** `window-schedule` also moves, by the window's length, the
  `closes_at` of each override of a published assignment that falls in the window (`closes_at >=
  starts_at AND closes_at < ends_at`), as one more CTE in the same statement, under the same
  exclusive lock, and audits `assignment.override_extended` per override as System (`studentId`,
  `closes_at` old and new, reason `maintenance`, `windowId`, `databaseRole`). The report gains
  `Overrides`, and the dry run counts them without moving any. The restricted role needs `SELECT,
  UPDATE (closes_at)` on the table, which R5's `grant_maintenance_role` adds; until then the
  extension runs under `MAINTENANCE_DATABASE_URL` as today. The maintenance tests run as the
  migrate role, which owns the table, so the restricted-role run is **not run** here; the
  statement's column list is the one the grant covers (`closes_at` is the only column it sets).
**Done when:**
- [x] (12a) `create_assignment_student_overrides`: `assignment_id` FK `ON DELETE CASCADE`, `student_id`
      FK users `ON DELETE CASCADE`, `closes_at timestamptz`, `duration_minutes integer` (1–600),
      `extra_attempts smallint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (0–10), `reason text NOT NULL` (1–500),
      `created_by` FK users `ON DELETE SET NULL`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `PRIMARY KEY
      (assignment_id, student_id)`, a CHECK that at least one of the three is set, and an index
      on `(student_id)`. App role: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE. Down drops the table
- [x] (12a) `POST /teacher/assignments/{id}/extend` (`teaching.assignments.write`): `{minutes
      (1–10080), notify}` moves `closes_at`; a closed assignment goes through the existing
      `reopenAssignment` instead (409 `ASSIGNMENT_CLOSED` here, a new `ErrorCode` value). Returns
      `Assignment`
- [x] (12a) `PUT /teacher/assignments/{id}/student-overrides` (`teaching.attempts.intervene`):
      `{studentIds (1–100), extendBy?, closesAt?, durationMinutes?, extraAttempts?, reason,
      notify}` upserts one row per student; a student who is not a target answers 422 naming
      them. `GET` on the same path returns the overrides and declares `x-resource-list`; `DELETE
      /teacher/assignments/{id}/student-overrides/{studentId}` removes one (both
      `teaching.attempts.intervene`). The dialogs carry a Reason field (T-R4.26)
- [x] (12a) The `x-resource` enum gains `override`. `{id}` declares `assignment`, the DELETE's
      `{studentId}` declares `override` and the PUT's `studentIds` declares `student`, so the
      isolation suite substitutes A's id into one of them at a time
- [x] (12b) Both operations recompute, under each attempt's row lock and in the same transaction,
      `deadline_at = least(started_at + effective duration, effective close)` for attempts in
      progress, only where the new value is later; each change is audited through the

- [x] (12a) Start and resume use the student's effective window, duration and attempt allowance; the
      student card and intro show the effective close; for that student alone, a closed
      assignment with an override still ahead reads as open
- [x] (12a) The override feeds `readerClose` only (`attempts/repositories/result.go`, which decides
      whether a student's result is withheld and when it is released); the class average stays

- [x] (12b) Monitor rows carry the override ("Extended to …"); R1's maintenance extension also moves

      (release-checklist hand-off)
- [x] (12a) Tests: unit tests for the effective window (`shared/schedule`), integration for a start

### T-R4.13 — Duplicate, item analysis and results export
**Depends on:** T-R4.7
**Touches:** `server/internal/modules/assignments/{application/command/duplicate.go (new),domain/{assignment,commands,repository}.go,http/{assignments,duplicate}.go,repositories/{assignments,duplicate}.go}`, `server/internal/modules/attempts/{application/{app.go,ports/zones.go,query/{item_analysis,results_export}.go,internal/support/service.go},domain/{analysis,repository}.go,http/{item_analysis,results_export}.go,repositories/analysis.go}`, `server/internal/platform/tabular/csv.go` (new), `server/internal/core/{wiring/wiring.go,router/ratelimits.go}`, `api/openapi.yaml`, generated code, `server/internal/core/router/tests/testdata/permissions.golden`, `server/tests/{isolation_cases,results_flow}_test.go`
**Size:** M
**As built (2026-10-10):**
- **No migration, no new `ErrorCode`.** The contract gains three operations, `DuplicateAssignmentInput`,
  `ItemAnalysis` and `ItemAnalysisItem`, an optional `Assignment.questionCount`, and on `listAssignments` the
  shared `q` parameter and `classId` as a list. The pins move: 113 operations to 116, 14 teacher lists to 15
  (item analysis, which declares `x-resource-list: version-question`), the Assistant's refusals stay 38 (it
  holds both permissions), and `PrincipalRateLimits()` gains `GET /teacher/assignments/results.csv`, 10 a
  minute and 60 an hour per actor (a heavy read that the plan did not limit; the 20000-row cap bounds one
  call, the limit bounds the calls). The permission golden gains the three lines and no other.
- **Duplicate** is one `INSERT … SELECT` from the original, so a column the copy must carry is named once.
  The copy pins the same test and version, keeps the time limit, attempts, both shuffle flags, the five review
  options (`release` and `showClassAverage` included), the integrity policy and the note to students, opens
  at the caller's `now` and lasts `closes_at - opens_at` of the original, is a draft (`published_at` NULL,
  `closed_at` NULL) owned by the caller, and is assigned to `classIds` and to no student by name. An early
  close, the original's overrides and its attempts are not copied. `classIds` may be empty. The original must be
  reachable (`visibility.AssignmentIDs`, or any under `scope.all`) or the answer is 404; its version must be
  one the caller may assign, by `publishedTestFor` as `createAssignment` applies it, so a teacher who reaches
  an assignment through a class and does not own its test answers 409 `TEST_NOT_PUBLISHED` (open question);
  a class the caller does not teach answers 400 on `classIds`, as an unknown one does. Audited as
  `assignment.duplicated` with `sourceId`.
- **Item analysis** ranges over papers, one per student the caller reaches (`visibility.Papers`): the
  student's latest attempt that is handed in (`submitted`, `timed_out`, `graded`) and not voided, so a retake
  replaces the first sitting, a voided retake changes nothing, and a paper still in progress is not counted.
  `handedIn` counts them. For each question of the pinned version, numbered across sections as the grading
  queue numbers them, `answered` counts the papers whose saved answer says something
  (`answered.SaysSomething`), and `correctRate` is the papers that earned the question's full points
  (`final_score >= points`, for an auto-marked and a manually marked answer alike) over the papers that have
  a mark for it. A question left unanswered counts against it; a manual answer with no mark yet counts for
  neither side; a question with no marked paper has a null rate and sorts last. Order: rate ascending, nulls
  last, then number. The prompt excerpt is the plain prompt with its whitespace collapsed, cut at 140
  characters. Overdue attempts are closed before the read, as the monitor does.
- **The export** writes the monitor's roster, not only the papers: one row per target student the caller
  reaches (the classes they teach, the individual targets they reach, everything under `scope.all`; a
  disabled student has none), so a student who has not started has a row with status "not started". The
  attempt that stands for a student is the monitor's: the latest that is not voided, else the latest. Class
  is the names of the target classes the caller reaches that the student belongs to, sorted and joined by
  `; ` (empty for a student named alone). Version is the number. Score, Max and Percent (one decimal) are
  filled when the attempt is handed in and no manual mark is outstanding, so a paper awaiting its teacher has
  none; Submitted is in the caller's own zone (`identity` `EffectiveZone` through a new `ports.Zones`, wired
  with `WithZones` as the dashboard's is), the header names the zone, and a zone that cannot be resolved falls
  back to `Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh` (a fixed +07:00 when tzdata is missing) instead of failing the download. Headers,
  status words and Có/Không follow `Accept-Language`, Vietnamese by default. Overdue attempts are closed first.
- **Limits and refusals.** *Departure from the plan's "streams":* the file is built in memory and written
  once, so the status is decided before the first byte; the bound that makes that safe is 20000 rows (the
  query reads one more to tell), a few megabytes at most. Over it is
  422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` with `details.ids`. `ids` is 1 to 50 unique uuids (the schema, so 400 otherwise);
  one id the caller does not reach, or that does not exist, is 404 for the whole request, as a missing one
  answers, and nothing is read. `Content-Disposition` is not in the CORS exposed headers (`httpx` is outside
  this task), so the SPA names the file itself.
- **`platform/tabular`** holds `EscapeCell` and a `CSV` writer (byte-order mark, CRLF, every cell escaped),
  with no dependency beyond the standard library. A cell whose first character after any leading spaces
  (space, no-break space, ideographic space) is `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return, or one of the
  four full-width forms `＝ ＋ － ＠`, gets a leading `'`; the first two columns of that list are the plan's, the
  rest the verification strategy's. R5 extends the package with XLSX.
- **`listAssignments`.** `classId` repeats (`?classId=a&classId=b`, at most 50) and keeps the assignments that
  target any of them; a class the caller does not teach contributes nothing, as before, and `EveryTarget` lifts
  that for a caller who holds `scope.all` and lists their own (T-R4.54's `Own()` is unchanged: it narrows
  who the rows belong to, the class filter narrows within them). `q` keeps the assignments whose test title,
  or the name of a target class the caller reaches, contains the text, accents and case ignored, with `%`,
  `_` and `\` literal; the facets follow `q` and the classes, as they followed `classId`. Every row, and every
  write's answer, carries `questionCount`, counted as `TestVersion.questionCount` counts.
- **Evidence.** Transport tests on the stubbed queries pin the file (BOM, CRLF, headers, both languages,
  escaping, zones, the 404 and 422 answers, the null rate); integration tests drive the real queries,
  including exactly 20000 rows and 20001; an end-to-end flow exports, analyses and duplicates through the
  router. 25 mutants were run, each killed by a named test: in `tabular` a leading `-` unescaped, leading
  spaces not skipped, a row written unescaped; UTC times in the export; in `Duplicate` a published copy, the
  note not copied, the original's dates, a fixed window length, an unreached original readable; in the list
  `q` unescaped and only the first class; in the analysis pending marks in the denominator, the oldest attempt,
  "more than full", a voided paper, every student's paper, an unmarked question first; in the export an
  unreached assignment skipped, the cap off by one in each direction, a voided attempt standing, a score on a
  paper with marks outstanding, a disabled student, overdue attempts not closed, and the export unlimited per
  actor. The canaries that apply, `publish_snapshot_test.go` and `TestTheSameClientSeqFromTwoSessionsBothPersist`
  with the rest of their packages, and the attempts unit tests, passed on the branch point `63b05880` and on
  the head; no web source changed, so the three web canaries were not run.
**Done when:**
- [x] `POST /teacher/assignments/{id}/duplicate` (`teaching.assignments.write`) `{classIds}`
      creates a draft with the same version, window length and rules; a duplicated draft opens
      now for the original's length; returns 201 `Assignment`
- [x] `GET /teacher/assignments/{id}/item-analysis` (`teaching.grading`) → `{handedIn, items:
      [{questionId, number, type, promptExcerpt (≤140), answered, correctRate|null}]}` sorted
      by `correctRate` ascending, nulls last. An auto-marked answer counts when correct; a
      manual one counts when it earned full points; an ungraded manual answer does not count.
      Item analysis declares `x-resource-list`
- [x] `GET /teacher/assignments/results.csv?ids=` (`teaching.grading`, ≤50 ids, ≤20000 rows)
      streams UTF-8 with a BOM and `Content-Disposition: attachment`; columns Assignment,
      Version, Class, Student, Email, Status, Score, Max, Percent, Submitted (actor's zone),
      Focus lost, Flagged
- [x] `listAssignments` gains `q`, `classId` as a list and `questionCount` on its items
- [x] `x-resource`: the duplicate's `classIds` body field declares `class`; the export's `ids`
      query declares `assignment`
- [x] Every cell goes through `tabular.EscapeCell`, which prefixes `'` to a value starting with
      `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or CR; R5 extends the package with XLSX
- [x] Tests: escape cases (`=HYPERLINK(…)`, `+1`, `-2+3`, `@SUM(A1)`), CSV integration, item
      analysis ordering, isolation for all three
- [x] The five canaries and the attempts module's unit tests run on the branch point and on the
      last commit, both runs in the PR (high-risk area)

### T-R4.16a — Test versions: publish base, usage counts and change note
**Depends on:** —
**Touches:** `migrations/00092_add_test_versions_change_note.sql`, `api/openapi.yaml` (`TestVersion`, `Test`, `publishTest`), generated code, `server/internal/modules/tests/{domain/{publish,test}.go,application/command/publish.go,repositories/{publish_publisher,publish_load,publish_groups,group_store,group_mutation,duplicate,store,versions,usage (new)}.go,http/{publish,tests}.go}`, `server/internal/shared/schedule/schedule.go` (new), `server/internal/modules/assignments/repositories/{assignments,reopen}.go`, `docs/plan/20-data-model.md` (D-32), the web fixtures the contract change forces
**Size:** M
**As built (2026-10-08):**
- **The migration is `00092`,** the next number after `00091` on `work/redesign-r4` at
  `5821b264`; renumber it if another file lands first. `change_note text` is nullable with no
  default, so the previous release's insert keeps working, and no row is backfilled. The check,
  `test_versions_change_note_check`, holds 1 to 200 characters and is part of the `ADD COLUMN`, so
  there is nothing to validate. The command trims the note and stores NULL for a blank one
  (`PublishManager.ChangeNote`), so the table never meets an empty string. D-32 records it.
- **`testUpdatedAt` is a field of `TestVersion`,** because `additionalProperties: false` rules
  out a wrapper, and only `publishTest`'s 201 carries it. It is the `RETURNING updated_at` of the
  `UPDATE app.tests` the publish already runs, so it is the value the `00015` trigger wrote
  inside the publish transaction. A save sent with it succeeds; a save sent with the pre-publish
  time answers `STALE_WRITE` (both are in `publish_version_metadata_test.go`).
  `domain.Version.TestUpdatedAt` is a pointer set only by `Publish`.
- **`publishTest`'s body is optional,** `{changeNote}` of at most 200 characters, null allowed,
  and the operation answers 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` for a longer note, a wrong type or an unknown
  field. No operation was added, so the permission golden, the operation count and the isolation
  table are unchanged.
- **`assignmentCount` counts every `app.assignments` row that names the version,** drafts
  included and whoever created them, through `assignments_version_idx`. `DeleteVersion` still
  relies on the foreign key's RESTRICT.
- **`Test.assignments {live, scheduled, closed}` is required on every `Test`:** the list rows and
  `getTest`, and so every write's answer. It counts the non-draft assignments that name any
  version of the test, by the derived status, with `live` as `open`. The status rule is one
  constant, `schedule.DerivedStatus` in `internal/shared/schedule`, moved there from
  `assignments/repositories` (its list, facets and reopen) and read by `tests/repositories/usage.go`
  as well, because a repository may not import another module's repository. **T-R4.11 and
  T-R4.12 change that window rule in that one place.**
  `TestATestCountsItsAssignmentsByDerivedStatus` covers an early close that has passed, one that
  is still ahead, a passed window, a scheduled one and a draft.
- **The loader is split.** `loadDraft` is gone. Publish and Duplicate call `lockDraftContent`
  (the row locks, in the order `loadDraft` took them) and then `loadDraftSnapshot(ctx, tx,
  testID)`, which only reads. `readLockedGroup` takes the lock clause
  (`"FOR SHARE OF g"`, `"FOR UPDATE OF g"` or none); the snapshot reads groups with none
  (`GroupsPostgres.readUnlocked`) and skips `lockGroupReadOwner`, because `lockDraftContent` has already share-locked every group of
  the test, the caller holds the test row, and `appendDraftGroup` still refuses a group of another
  section. `GroupsPostgres.Get` is unchanged. T-R4.16b adds `loadVersion` and the read-only diff
  transaction on top of it.
- **One existing test changed:** `publish_history_test.go` compared the publish answer with the
  history row by value; it now clears `TestUpdatedAt` on its copy first. `publish_snapshot_test.go`
  and `publish_lockorder_test.go` are untouched.
**Done when:**
- [x] `add_test_versions_change_note`: `change_note text` (≤200), nullable. Down drops it
- [x] `publishTest` takes an optional `{changeNote}` and its response gains `testUpdatedAt`, the
      test row's `updated_at` after the publish transaction, so the builder can keep saving
      without `STALE_WRITE`
- [x] `TestVersion` gains `assignmentCount` and `changeNote`; list rows gain `assignments
      {live, scheduled, closed}` counts for the card's "2 live" line
- [x] `loadDraft` is split into `lockDraftContent` and `loadDraftSnapshot`, with the lock clause
      an argument of `readLockedGroup`; Publish and Duplicate keep their
      locks and their order
- [x] Tests: the change note (trim, blank, absent, the history), `testUpdatedAt` against a
      following save, both counts per derived status, the constraint at its bounds with the
      previous release's insert still valid, the transport and the router contract;
      `publish_snapshot_test.go` green and untouched

### T-R4.16b — Test versions: the diff and unpublished changes
**Depends on:** T-R4.16a
**Touches:** `api/openapi.yaml`, generated code, `server/internal/modules/tests/{domain/{diff,diff_paper,diff_context,diff_request}.go (new),domain/{draft,repository,test}.go,application/{app.go,query/diff.go (new),query/get.go},repositories/{diff.go (new, holds loadVersion),store.go,usage.go,list.go,group_frozen.go,publish_groups.go,version_group_restore.go},http/{diff.go (new),tests.go}}`, `server/internal/core/router/tests/{permissions_test.go,testdata/permissions.golden}`, `server/tests/isolation_cases_test.go`, the web fixtures the contract change forces
**Size:** M
**As built (2026-10-08):**
- **No migration.** The contract is `getTestVersionDiff` (`workspace.teacher`, `x-resource` the
  test) and `Test.unpublishedChanges`, required and nullable. The permission golden and the
  operation count (108 to 109) move with it, and the isolation table has its row beside
  `deleteTestVersion`.
- **The read is one `REPEATABLE READ`, `READ ONLY` transaction** (`Postgres.DiffPapers`): `Begin`,
  then `SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY` as the first statement, so
  both papers are one snapshot, then 16a's lock-free `loadDraftSnapshot` and `loadVersion`. It
  cannot run on a repository built over a transaction (`Begin` there is a savepoint), and its doc
  comment says so. No `db.Conn` gained a `BeginTx`. `readFrozenGroup` takes the lock clause
  (`versionShared` for `Frozen` and the restore, `versionUnlocked` here); the draft's
  "group belongs to another section" became the sentinel `errGroupOtherSection`, with the same
  text, so the diff can tell it from a database failure.
- **Sides.** `against=previous` is the highest version below the path version and, on the first
  version, has no `from`: every question is `added` and the total is not reported. `draft` makes
  the path version `from`. A number puts the lower of the two first. The path version itself is a
  400 from `domain.ParseAgainst`, a missing version or another teacher's test a 404 (one message
  for both, so a stranger learns nothing), and an unreadable paper a 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED`
  (the draft's groups with `against=draft`, or a version's, see below), the answer a refused
  group gets elsewhere.
- **Identity.** `Compare` matches by `SourceID` (the version's `source_question_id`, the draft's
  bank id; group members too), pairing equal keys in paper order, then the questions left over by a
  fingerprint of their type, prompt, explanation, media with its audio policy and transcript,
  points, options, blanks and answer key. Restore as draft copies every question and renews every
  gap id, so the fingerprint reads a gap by its place in the text and a blank's gap by that place;
  a group's bindings are read through the pairing, not through the ids. A restored version is
  therefore identical to its source, and a restored question that was then edited is not matched:
  it reads as `added` and `removed`.
- **Entries.** `added` and `removed` per question (`removed` numbered in the older paper);
  `changed` per question with the parts that differ (`type`, `prompt`, `options`, `blanks`,
  `media`, `points`, `explanation`, `context`, `section`); `answer` per question whose key differs;
  `points` once when the totals differ. A question can have a `changed` and an `answer` entry.
  Level, skill, tags and the order of questions are not compared.
- **The key is read by position,** as the letters the teacher sees: correct options by place,
  each blank's accepted answers sorted, and the sample answer. A key that stays on A while the
  options swap is `changed` (options) alone; one that moves with its option is `changed` and
  `answer` (`answerFrom`, `answerTo` carry the labels, for a choice question only).
- **`context` and `section` reach every member.** A group's title, instructions, materials with
  their gap bindings and recordings, and a member's option order, are the question's `context`; a
  section's title and instructions are its questions' `section`. So editing a passage changes each
  question of the group, and renaming a section each question in it. That is one entry per question
  as the contract has it, and it inflates `unpublishedChanges` for a large group or section.
  The Principal kept it in review round 1: the deck's banner is boolean, and T-R4.30's chips need
  one entry per member.
- **`unpublishedChanges`** is `len(changes)` of the latest version against the draft, counted in a
  second read-only transaction after the one that read the test (`GetHandler`). It is skipped for a
  test whose `current_version` is 0. It is null for a test with no version, in every list row, in
  a write's answer, and when a group graph is refused: the draft's (`ErrDraftUnreadable`: a
  `GroupError`, an unknown question, a group of another section) or the latest version's (below);
  a draft with no sections is an empty paper, so its questions are all `removed`; any other failure
  of the count fails `getTest`.
- **A version that no longer reads back** (review round 1). `loadVersion` reads a group with
  `readFrozenGroup`, which validates it. A stored group that fails (`*GroupError`) or is gone
  (`ErrNotFound`) is wrapped in `domain.ErrVersionUnreadable`, so `getTest` leaves the count null
  and the endpoint answers 422 `VALIDATION_FAILED` with its own message, not a 500 and not a 404
  that would say the version does not exist. A test freezes a group, shifts its members' ordinals
  in the database, and shows `getTest` at 200 with null, the diff against the draft and against
  the previous version unreadable and not "not found", and the count back once mended.
- **No error is read as a value.** `Compare`, `Introduction` and `DiffPapers.Changes` return an
  error: a question's points that `PointUnits` refuses (stored data never has them) no longer
  count as 0, and a failure to encode a part of a question no longer gives an empty part. The
  handlers pass it up as a 500.
- **Cost.** A diff is `14 + S` statements for a draft of S sections, begin and commit included, and
  16 more for each group: a 10 and a 100 question paper in five sections both run 19, and one
  group of two members with a standalone question runs 31. So it is constant in the questions and
  linear in the groups, as publish's own read is (`readUnlocked` and `readFrozenGroup` each read
  one group in about eight statements); batching them is not done here.
- **QA-16a-1 is fixed in this PR** (its own commit): `testColumns` carried seven correlated
  subplans, four over the draft's questions and, since 16a, three over the assignments. At
  `limit=100` the planner priced the list at 111,350 on 400 tests, 1,200 versions and 3,600
  assignments, over `jit_above_cost`, so a connection's first executions JIT-compiled 168
  functions (98.7 ms against 6.9 ms). Both groups are now one `LATERAL` aggregate each
  (`testFrom`); the estimate is 65,541, there is no JIT, and the 100 rows are identical. The
  laterals run for the rows an `OFFSET` skips too, as the subplans did; at page sizes of at most
  100 that is small.
  Folding only the assignments left it at 100,083, still over.
- **Read-only evidence.** 50 diffs and 25 `getTest` against a scratch database left
  `pg_stat_user_tables.n_tup_upd`, `n_tup_ins` and `n_tup_del` (summed over schema `app`) and
  `tests.updated_at` unchanged, where one `UPDATE` of the test moved them (a throwaway test, not
  kept: the counters are database-wide and other packages write). The suite pins the same by the
  test row's `xmin`, `xmax` and `updated_at`, by the statements the transaction runs (the second
  is the `SET TRANSACTION`; none is a write or has a `FOR` clause), and by a bank edit committed
  from another connection in the middle of a diff, which it must not see.
**Done when:**
- [x] `domain/diff.go` compares two papers, matching questions by `source_question_id` (group
      members too), then, for what is left unmatched, by identical content, because Restore as
      draft creates new bank questions: `added`, `removed`, `changed` (prompt, options, media,
      blanks, points, explanation, context, section), `answer` (correct options or accepted answers) and `points` (total); order
      alone is not a change
- [x] `GET /teacher/tests/{id}/versions/{version}/diff?against=previous|draft|<n>` (the
      `x-permission` of `listTestVersions`) → `{from, to, changes: [{kind, questionNumber,
      questionId?, params}]}` (DG-66)
- [x] `getTest` gains `unpublishedChanges`, the number of changes between the draft and the
      latest version, computed by the same diff in a `REPEATABLE READ READ ONLY` transaction that
      reads the draft with `loadDraftSnapshot` and never calls `lockDraftContent`,
      `GroupsPostgres.Get` or any `FOR` clause; it is null in a list row, in a write's answer,
      for a test with no version, and when the draft's group graph is refused
- [x] Tests: diff unit tests for every kind and for reorder; integration for the endpoint and for
      a bank edit that makes the draft differ; `publish_snapshot_test.go` green and untouched
- [x] QA-16a-1: the list's estimated cost at `limit=100` is under `jit_above_cost`, with the
      same rows

### T-R4.17a — Media library backend
**Depends on:** —
**Touches:** `migrations/NNNNN_add_media_assets_library_fields.sql`, `migrations/NNNNN_add_media_assets_dimensions.sql`, `migrations/NNNNN_widen_media_assets_audio_bytes.sql`, `api/openapi.yaml`, generated code, `server/internal/modules/media/{domain/asset.go,domain/manager.go,application/command/upload.go,application/command/update.go (new),application/query/list.go,repositories/store.go,repositories/list.go,repositories/references.go,http/media.go}`, `server/internal/platform/config/` (`tests/deployment_test.go` among them), `fly.toml`, `.env.example`, `web/src/features/media/{limits,probe}.ts`, `web/tests/units/contract/openapi-contract.test.ts`
**Size:** M
**As built:**
- **Touched beyond the list:** in the module, `domain/{commands,errors,repository}.go`,
  `application/{app.go,ports/ports.go,query/{total_bytes,facets,usage}.go}` and
  `application/internal/support/{service,shared,intake}.go`; `platform/probe/image.go`,
  `core/adapters/probe.go`, `core/wiring/media.go`; the pinned router tests (the golden, the
  count 102 to 103, the Assistant's refusals 33 to 34, the body budgets 8 to 9),
  `server/tests/isolation_{cases,flow}_test.go`, and `platform/db/tests/constraints_test.go`,
  whose oversized-asset case named the constraint 00084 replaces and skips without a database.
  New tests: `core/router/tests/media_test.go`,
  `core/tests/media_wiring_test.go`, `media/application/tests/{intake,library}_test.go`,
  `platform/probe/tests/image_test.go`, `server/tests/media_library_flow_test.go`. Web:
  `MaterialAssetDialog.tsx`, the three audio sentences, `upload-panel.test.tsx`, two MSW
  fixtures, and two new tests (`media/limit-strings.test.ts`,
  `question-groups/material-asset-limit.test.tsx`).
- **The migrations are `00082` to `00084`,** the next numbers after `00081` at the branch point.
  If another file lands first they are renamed, in that order, and nothing else changes.
- **One predicate is the library:** `a.deleted_at IS NULL AND a.replaced_by IS NULL`, then the
  scope's owner, in `repositories/list.go` (`library`). The list, `total`, `totalBytes`, the
  facets, the usage and the quota sum are built on it. `Get`, `Readable`, `ReferencesFor`,
  `LockForVersionUse` and `SoftDelete` are unchanged and still reach a replaced file. Nothing
  here writes `replaced_by`.
- **`questionCount` and `unused` are one subquery** (`questionsUsing`, `references.go`): the
  questions that attach the file, and the members of the groups whose recordings or stimulus
  assets name it, as a `UNION`, so a question counts once. A group member cannot be
  soft-deleted (`questions_context_complete`), so "live member" is every member. Any owner's
  question counts; the files counted are the scope's own.
- **The quota is two functions that take a `pgx.Tx`:** `repositories.RequireQuota(ctx, tx,
  QuotaCheck{OwnerID, Add, Quota, LeaveOut})` takes
  `pg_advisory_xact_lock(73820, hashtext(owner::uuid::text))` and then sums the owner's library
  without `LeaveOut`; `repositories.Insert(ctx, tx, in)` writes the row and its audit row for
  `in.OwnerID`, or the uploader when that is empty. `Postgres.Insert` composes them. The one
  comparison, `held + add > quota`, is `domain.Assets.CheckQuota`.
- **The command also checks before it stores the object,** from `Repo.Usage`, so a full library
  costs no 50 MiB put. That read takes no lock; the check inside the insert is the one that
  holds, and a refusal there deletes the object.
- **`InsertInput` gained `OwnerID`, `DisplayName`, `DefaultMaxPlays`, `Width`, `Height` and
  `QuotaBytes`.** A caller of the repository must set `QuotaBytes`: zero refuses every row. The
  application passes its own, `domain.DefaultOwnerQuotaBytes` unless `WithOwnerQuota` set
  another; a config test holds that default equal to `MEDIA_OWNER_QUOTA_MIB`'s 5120.
- **The intake helper is `support.Service.Receive(body io.Reader) (*Intake, error)`:** it copies
  at most the audio limit to a temp file through one 32 KiB buffer, identifies the file, refuses
  an image over its own limit and audio over five minutes, and reads an image's size. `Intake`
  carries the kind, MIME type, bytes, duration, size in pixels and checksum; `Body()` rewinds
  and `Close()` removes the file. It takes nothing from the upload command.
- **One code, two sentences for a file too large.** `domain.ErrImageTooLarge` wraps
  `ErrTooLarge`; the transport words 10 MB for it and 50 MB otherwise, and gives the 50 MB
  answer to `*http.MaxBytesError`, whether the cap is passed while a part before the file is
  skipped or while the file is copied.
- **`updateMedia` is a scoped read, then a scoped write.** A play limit depends on the kind, so
  the command reads the file first (`Repo.Find`, the library in the caller's scope) and the
  write repeats the predicate. The name is checked before the read and the play limit after it,
  so a foreign id and a missing one answer alike whatever the body holds. `defaultMaxPlays` is
  `json.RawMessage` in the generated type: left out keeps the limit, `null` clears it, and
  `null` is accepted for an image.
- **`defaultMaxPlays` is stored and applied to nothing.** No question takes it by itself; the
  editor reads it (T-R4.65). `uploadMedia` still answers `MediaAsset`, so the limit sent with an
  upload is read back from the library row.
- **`probe.Image`** reads PNG and JPEG through `image.DecodeConfig` and accepts only those two
  formats from it, whatever other decoder a later import registers; WebP is read from its first
  30 bytes. A header it cannot read, or a size that is not positive, stores the image without
  one. Files uploaded before 00083 have no size: there is no backfill.
- **`MEDIA_OWNER_QUOTA_MIB`** is 1 to 1048576 and is read whether or not object storage is
  configured, so a bad value stops the server either way.
- **The table refuses 51 MiB of audio.** The brief's test list says it accepts it; the rule in
  the same brief is 50 MiB, and the tests pin 50 MiB accepted, one byte more refused, and
  11 MiB of audio accepted, which 00010 refused.
- **The timeouts stay unchanged.** The streamed-body read deadline is 110 seconds; the
  server's 120-second write timeout bounds the response, not the request context. A 50 MiB
  payload needs at least 3.81 Mbit/s before multipart overhead, with more needed to leave
  time for probing, object storage and the database. `docs/setup/dns.md` records that limit
  and the separately verified Cloudflare defaults (100 MB body, 125-second proxy read,
  30-second proxy write). The size cap does not promise success on a slow connection.
**Done when:**
- [x] `add_media_assets_library_fields`: `display_name text` (1–200), `default_max_plays
      smallint` (NULL = not set, so new questions get the editor's 2; 0 = unlimited; 1–3; audio
      only), `replaced_by uuid REFERENCES app.media_assets(id) ON DELETE SET NULL` with
      `replaced_by <> id`. Down drops the three
- [x] `add_media_assets_dimensions`: `width`, `height` integers, images only, both or neither.
      Down drops both
- [x] `widen_media_assets_audio_bytes`: the bytes CHECK becomes audio ≤ 50 MiB, images ≤ 10 MiB
      (DG-63), added `NOT VALID` then validated, the old constraint dropped. Down re-adds the old
      CHECK `NOT VALID` so it works when larger audio exists
- [x] Upload accepts audio up to 50 MiB while formats and duration stay as spec §11.1 has them
      (MP3/M4A, five minutes) until the open item is answered; the body streams to a temp file,
      never into memory; images record their dimensions: PNG and JPEG with `image.DecodeConfig`,
      WebP from its header.
      `web/src/features/media/limits.ts`, `probe.ts` and the contract test travel in this pull
      request
- [x] `MEDIA_OWNER_QUOTA_MIB` (default 5120) is enforced per owner at upload under a
      transaction advisory lock keyed by owner, namespace 73820 (409 `MEDIA_QUOTA_EXCEEDED`,
      which is new); the quota check and the insert are functions that take a transaction, so
      T-R4.17b runs them inside its own;
      `.env.example` updated; `deployment_test.go` and `fly.toml` take `MEDIA_OWNER_QUOTA_MIB`
- [x] Contract: `LibraryAsset` gains `displayName`, `defaultMaxPlays`, `width`, `height` and
      `questionCount` (bank and draft references); `listMedia` gains `unused=true` and returns
      `usage {audioBytes, imageBytes, quotaBytes}`; replaced assets leave the listing; upload
      takes an optional `defaultMaxPlays` as a query parameter, and `uploadMedia` declares 400
      and 409; `PATCH /teacher/media/{id}`
      (`content.media.write`) sets `displayName` and `defaultMaxPlays`
- [x] `listMedia` gains `q`, matched without accents on the display name and the original
      filename through `app.immutable_unaccent(lower(…))`, and returns `facets {all, audio,
      image, unused}` counted under `q`, ignoring `kind` and `unused`. The Media page's tabs
      (T-R4.35) and the kind filter of "Choose from Media" (T-R4.65) read them, so a count
      follows the search (DG-17, DG-112)
- [x] Tests: rename, two concurrent uploads at the quota edge (one 409), the unused filter, 51 MiB
      audio answers 413, dimensions stored, an R3-shaped insert succeeds, the facets under a
      search with and without accents
- [ ] The five canaries and the media module's unit tests run on the branch point and on the

### T-R4.17b — Media replace
**Depends on:** T-R4.17a
**Touches:** `server/internal/modules/media/{application/command/replace.go (new),repositories/replace.go (new),http/media.go}`, `server/internal/shared/content/assets.go`, `api/openapi.yaml`, generated code
**Size:** M
**As built (T-R4.17b: media replace):**

- **API:** `replaceMedia`, `POST /teacher/media/{id}/replace`, declares
  `content.media.write` and the media resource. Multipart file validation retains
  upload's sniff, size, duration and probe limits; different kinds return
  415 `MEDIA_KIND_MISMATCH`. The closed 201 response holds the library asset and
  four nonnegative counts. Scoped preflight precedes multipart consumption;
  foreign and missing IDs answer identically, and a nil application answers 501.
  No legacy alias, catalogue or general Student asset shape changed.
- **Graph:** locks are sorted groups, sorted questions, old asset, then owner quota.
  Post-wait owner, current metadata and library eligibility are rechecked. Explicit
  locked owner and acting uploader remain distinct. Validated copied semantic IDs,
  recordings and relational holders change only inside the retained owner graph,
  with one revision per changed group and distinct counts. Archived live parents
  remain eligible; soft-deleted parents are excluded. Foreign bindings, published
  versions, import reviews and later bindings keep the readable old file. No test
  timestamp or snapshot changes.
- **Transaction and storage:** the repository requires a nonnil concrete
  `pgxpool.Pool` before Begin and owns the outer transaction. Nil Commit confirms
  Committed; known rollback or `SafeToRetry` confirms NotCommitted; other failures
  remain Unknown. Body failure performs independent bounded rollback. Fresh-key
  deletion follows Put failure or confirmed NotCommitted, synchronously with its
  own five-second context; Unknown, unrecognized errors and Committed failures
  retain it. Rollback/delete failures take internal-error precedence. Post-commit
  describe/count/sign failure can return 500, and retrying the old ID can return
  404. There is no distributed atomicity, idempotent retry, sweeper or ten-second
  guarantee for whole-server reclamation; failed Put compensation may race remote
  completion and Unknown may retain an orphan.
- **Contract and schema:** no migration, index, dependency or config change.
  Existing 00082–00084 fields and the quota helper are reused. OpenAPI was edited
  first; repeated `make gen` stays byte-identical and 132 contract cases pass.
  Permission, isolation and request-budget inventories add this operation.
  Generic database/storage helpers and prior canaries stay unchanged.
- **Developer evidence:** 510 passing integration events and 40 exclusive E2E
  events, 75 web checks across ten files including the real chunk build, strict
  project typecheck, build/vet/staticcheck and pinned lint pass. Fifteen actual
  assertion mutants were caught and restored. The initial overlapping-writer E2E
  failure remains an orchestration failure; no frozen lifecycle assertion changed.
  Principal's local-prefix finding was repaired only in two setup helpers; all
  25 replacement top-level scenarios produced 37 passing events on new non-task-
  prefixed PG18.6/MinIO resources. These event counts include subtests. Full old
  controls stayed identical and exact owned resources are absent. The rejected
  audit-row repair was not retried. Source review approves the repaired helpers;
  independent purpose-resource QA and exact-head CI remain integration gates.
- **Consumers:** T-R4.35 owns cache invalidation and question/group editor reloads,
  and must not automatically retry a possibly committed 500. Backend evidence
  does not certify a native media screen, builder, D4 or release.

**Done when:**
- [x] `POST /teacher/media/{id}/replace` (`content.media.write`, multipart `{file}`) validates
      like upload, requires the same kind (415 `MEDIA_KIND_MISMATCH`, which is new), and returns
      201 `{asset, repointed: {questions, groups}, left: {questions, groups}}`
- [x] In one transaction that locks the owner's groups, then the questions to re-point, then
      the old asset (`LockForVersionUse`), the order publish and a group save follow: the new
      asset takes the old display name and default play limit; the old gets `replaced_by`; bank
      questions and bank or draft-owned group media owned by the asset's owner are re-pointed;
      other owners' rows keep the old asset, which stays served, and are counted in `left`; no
      `test_version_*` row is touched; the change is audited with the counts (DG-09). The quota
      is summed for the old asset's owner without the old file. An import review draft is not
      re-pointed. A draft
      references an asset in recording rows and inside stored content, not only in
      `group_assets.go`
- [x] The old asset keeps serving published versions and attempts; deleting an asset a published
      version uses stays refused (409, as today)
- [x] Tests: a new integration test beside the canary shows a published version still delivers
      the old asset after a replace; a race of replace against publish ends with the version
      holding one of the two ids, never a dangling one; another owner's question bound to the
      asset (through `scope.all`) still holds the old asset after the owner replaces it;
      `TestLockForVersionUseSerialisesAgainstDelete` and `publish_snapshot_test.go` green and
      untouched

**As built (T-R4.19)**

- Both `getDashboard` and `getTeacherSummary` pass `.Own()` for teachers and admins;
  repositories retain `All`, and `ListAttempts` retains its full scope.
- `takingNow` counts distinct students and assignments before their live attempt deadlines.
- `submissions` returns exactly 7, 14 or 30 local calendar days, zero-filled and oldest
  first; only this reading changes with `range` (default `14d`).
- Submitted, timed-out and graded papers count by `submitted_at`; the integer average
  rounds half up over scored graded papers. Nullable graded papers still count and
  remain unchanged; the average is null when no scored graded paper exists.
- `today` is the first 20 published openings/effective closings in the local day;
  `notSubmitted` is distinct enabled reached targets with no handed-in paper.
- `recentActivity` is the ten newest attempt states or student-like code joins;
  each attempt has one event, voided attempts have none, and attempt flags carry through.
- `TeacherSummary.liveAssignments` matches the open Assignments list;
  `answersToGrade` counts unmarked manual answers only with `teaching.grading`, otherwise
  null without running that count. Notifications are the caller's own unread count.
- `ports.Zones` resolves the calendar name, `adapters.DefaultZone` supplies
  `Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh` until T-R4.7, and `Service.Now` is the injected new-query clock.
- `ports.UnreadNotifications` is the notifications summary handler, set through
  `WithNotifications`; a missing port answers 501. `New(repo)` stays unchanged.
- Legacy fields and their SQL clocks stay until their consumers move in T-R4.23/R5;
  T-R4.23 also switches the shell to `getTeacherSummary`. The existing dashboard client
  passes an empty query object for strict compilation after adding optional `range`,
  preserving its default and runtime URL. The Admin nav's grading count
  is their own reach while `ListAttempts` still reaches every teacher with `scope.all`.
- No migration or index was added. PostgreSQL 18 plans on 3,200 rollback-owned attempts
  used existing live and assignment/status indexes for selected readings and
  chose sequential scans for others; these measured choices are not scale certification.
- Public unit/integration tests cover permissions, ownership, clock/zone, nullable scores,
  padding, boundaries, target de-duplication and activity identity; HTTP isolation reads
  both operations. The original home scope case now pins `.Own()`; list scope stays wide.

### T-R4.21 — Imports history read model
**Depends on:** —
**Touches:** `migrations/00089_add_word_import_draft_counts.sql`, `server/internal/modules/imports/{domain/import.go,http/imports.go,repositories/list.go,repositories/drafts.go}` and public tests, `api/openapi.yaml`, generated code, the imports history page and its tests
**Size:** S
**Capacity decision:** INTEGER replaces the proposed SMALLINT because valid
existing drafts can exceed32767 derived findings. No new draft limit, clamping
or invented NULL is introduced. Counts are centralized in repository body writes,
so worker completion and review commands keep their existing interfaces.
**As built and verified:** The API, persistence and rebuilt history wiring are
complete. Migration 00089 adds nullable nonnegative INTEGER counts without a
backfill or default; pending candidates retain current counts until adoption.
On a fresh purpose PostgreSQL 18 database, 89 up/down/up preserves the legacy
body and run, 32768 remains exact, and all 18 named repository parents pass across
app and migrate connections. A genuine post-insert connection fault verifies
checked fixture cleanup. The purpose database was dropped normally after joins;
all six read-only control databases and append-only ACLs remained unchanged.
The earlier metadata-harness failure remains recorded separately.

Independent native verification passed 25 cases with no retry and personally
reviewed 20 history boards at 360/768/1024/1280/1440, both themes and both sidebar
states. It covers all six filters, four Processing statuses, nullable/zero/large
chips, authoritative search counters, classic-scrollbar geometry, skeleton
hover/reduced motion and actual menu keyboard focus. These native fixtures do
not claim live API or cookie coverage. Independent source review and exact
product-head CI 37510861704 pass. This task does not authorize a release.
**Done when:**
- [x] `add_word_import_draft_counts`: `open_action_count`, `open_confirm_count integer` (≥0),
      nullable; written by the new binary on every draft write (processing result, review save,
      adopt) from `domain.Review.Summary` (`Blocking`, `NeedsDecision`). Down drops both
- [x] `listWordImports`: `status` becomes an array (a single value still works); the response
      gains `facets {all, processing, needsReview, failed, committed, cancelled}`, where
      processing covers `awaiting_sources`, `queued`, `processing` and `committing`; the counts
      follow the search and ignore the status filter, as the `TestStatusFacets` schema's counts
      do; items gain `reviewCounts {needsAction, toConfirm}|null`
- [x] Web call sites change in the same PR
- [x] Tests: facet counts under a search, counts written by review save and by the worker, null
      for a draft untouched since the deploy

### T-R4.22 — Rotate every legacy join code at release
**Depends on:** T-R4.10a, R2's join-code encryption
**Touches:** `server/internal/modules/classes/application/command/rotate_legacy.go` (new), `server/internal/modules/classes/repositories/joincodes.go`, `server/internal/core/jobs/join_codes.go` (new), `server/internal/core/core.go`, `server/internal/core/wiring/classes.go`, `server/internal/core/jobs/tests/join_codes_test.go` (new), `docs/plan/20-data-model.md` (advisory-lock registry)
**Size:** M
**As built:**
- **Touched beyond the list:** `classes/domain/{commands.go,repository.go}`,
  `classes/application/{app.go,ports/ports.go (new)}`, `core/wiring/wiring.go` (the notifier
  argument, `Assembly.Classes` and the import its type needs), `core/tests/jobs_test.go`,
  `docs/quizzivy-spec-v0.3.md` (§6.1, v0.50) and three test files:
  `classes/application/tests/rotate_legacy_test.go` (the command, untagged),
  `classes/application/tests/joincode_legacy_rotation_test.go` (integration) and
  `server/tests/legacy_join_codes_flow_test.go` (the start-up path end to end, and the only test
  that sees the notifier wired).
- **One class is one transaction of six statements,** in `Postgres.RotateLegacyCode`:
  `SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(73819, 41)`; `SELECT true FROM app.classes WHERE id = $1 FOR NO
  KEY UPDATE`; the class's active code read again `FOR UPDATE` under `revoked_at IS NULL AND
  lookup_scheme = 1 AND expires_at > $now`; `UPDATE … SET revoked_at = $now` on that row; the
  `INSERT` of the sealed row, with the columns `Rotate` writes; the audit `INSERT`. With no class
  row or no code row the transaction commits having written nothing and the method answers
  false. That second read is what makes two start-ups rotate once.
- **The new row** keeps the old row's `expires_at`, `max_uses` (a NULL stays NULL) and
  `created_by`, starts at `uses_count` 0 and is created at the `$now` the old row is revoked at.
  `classes.self_join_enabled` is not written: the class row is locked and never updated.
- **The lock order is the class row and then the code row, and the reverse of a redemption's,**
  which locks the code row and then takes a key-share lock on the class row through the member
  insert. As first built the class row was locked `FOR UPDATE`, which conflicts with that
  key-share lock, so the two could deadlock, and a teacher's `Rotate` and `Revoke` still could.
  F-23 found it as an intermittent failure: when the database chose the rotation as the victim,
  its one retry started at once, took the class row before the woken redemption had taken its
  key-share lock back, and met the same cycle. The class row is now locked `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` by
  the legacy rotation, `Rotate` and `Revoke`, which a key-share lock does not wait for. The
  modes, the orders, what stays serialised and why `Delete` keeps `FOR UPDATE` are in
  `20-data-model.md` §33, "Lock order for a class and its join codes". The repository still
  answers `domain.ErrRotationContended` for SQLSTATE 40P01 and 40001, read from the
  `pgconn.PgError` code, and the command still tries such a class once more, after every other
  class and with a new code, and counts it in `Failed` if that fails too. No other error is
  retried.
- **The audit row:** actor NULL, `class.join_code_rotated`, entity `class_join_code`, entity id
  the new row, diff `{"reason": "legacy_rotation", "previousHint", "hint"}` and nothing else.
- **The old code answers through today's code paths, with no special case.** Where self-join is
  open it answers `PreviewRevoked` (`JOIN_CODE_REVOKED`), as a code its teacher replaced with
  `Rotate` does. Where self-join is closed or the class archived it answers `PreviewInvalid`, as
  an unknown code does, before the rotation and after it, and so does the new code of that class
  until its teacher opens it.
- **The command** lists once (`Postgres.LegacyCodeClasses`: by teacher, then name, archived
  classes included, no lock), groups by teacher and answers `domain.LegacyRotation{Found,
  Rotated, Failed, Teachers, NotifyFailed}`. A class another run rotated first is in `Found`
  only. It takes two optional callbacks, `Found(count)` and `Problem(err)`, because the
  application layer does not log: the job logs from them.
- **The notification:** one `Notify` per teacher, after that teacher's last class, or after the
  retries when one of their classes was contended. Kind `join_codes.rotated`, params `{count,
  classNames}` (the first five rotated, in name order), target `classes`, dedupe key
  `join_codes.rotated:legacy`, merge `Add`. Two runs that split a teacher's classes leave one
  row, unread again, with the summed count and the later run's names. A class without a teacher
  is rotated and nobody is told.
- **A teacher can be left untold.** A failed `Notify` is counted and logged and undoes nothing. A
  process that dies between a teacher's last commit and their `Notify` leaves them untold: the
  next run finds nothing to rotate and sends nothing. The release note tells teachers too.
  Each `Notify` runs under a ten-second budget of its own, detached from the run's context, so a
  run cut short by its two minutes or by shutdown rotates no further class and still tells each
  teacher of the classes it had rotated; the run can outlive its budget by up to ten seconds for
  each teacher still owed one. At shutdown that is best effort, since nobody waits for the job
  and the pool closes behind the server: a `Notify` that fails there is counted in
  `notify_failed`, and the next run's notification then counts only the classes it rotates.
- **The job** `jobs.RotateLegacyJoinCodes` runs once under a two-minute budget, started by
  `Serve` beside the prunes and by no other process, and never on a timer. It logs
  `legacy_join_codes_found {count}` before the first class when the count is above zero and
  `legacy_join_codes_rotated {found, rotated, failed, teachers, notify_failed}` after the last;
  a warning `legacy_join_codes_problem {err}` for each class left unrotated and each teacher
  left untold, naming the class or the user and never a code or a hint; and
  `legacy_join_codes_failed {err}` when the listing fails. A run that finds nothing logs nothing.
- **A class left behind waits for the next start-up:** a contended one that failed again on its
  retry, one that failed for any other reason (tried once), or one the budget did not reach. The
  release check runs the count query again after the deploy.
- **The port** is `classes/application/ports.Notifier`, the notifications module's `Notify`
  handler, set with `Application.WithNotifier`. `New` keeps its signature.
- **The tests narrow the listing, not the command.** The command rotates every legacy code it
  finds, and the integration tier runs packages in parallel against one database, so those tests
  run the real command over a repository whose listing keeps only the test's own classes. The
  races are staged with held locks and `pg_blocking_pids`, never with a sleep: a holder of
  advisory key 41, a `FOR UPDATE` on the row the new code's `created_by` points at (which stops
  the rotation, or a teacher's `Rotate`, at its insert), a `FOR SHARE` on the class's code row
  (which stops the rotation at its second read), and an uncommitted account under the joining
  student's email (which stops a redemption between its code lock and its member insert). One
  deadlock round is staged the same way. A writer that holds the code row closes the cycle by
  asking for the class row once the rotation has waited 50 ms, or half of `deadlock_timeout`
  (read from `pg_settings`) if that is less, the one wait on the clock; the rotation's timer
  started first, so it fires first and the rotation is the victim; a holder queued for advisory key 41 behind the rotation takes the key when the
  rotation aborts and keeps the retry out until the writer has finished, so the retry cannot take
  the class row ahead of it. A redemption holding the code row is tested against the legacy
  rotation, `Rotate` and `Revoke` in turn, each in its only attempt, and a `Rotate` in flight is
  tested to hold back `Revoke`, a rename and the legacy rotation (`joincode_lock_order_test.go`).
- **"The R3 binary redeems the new code" is proven by reading:** a test compares, column by
  column, the row the rotation writes with a row a teacher's `Rotate` wrote, and v0.9.0 has read
  scheme 2 since R2.
**Done when:**
- [x] `RotateLegacyJoinCodes`: for each class with an active legacy (hashed) code — not
      revoked, not expired — one transaction revokes it and issues an encrypted code under the
      current key id, with the same `expires_at` and `max_uses` (a stored NULL stays NULL),
      `uses_count` 0 and self-join unchanged; audited as System with reason `legacy_rotation`,
      the diff carrying hints only (DG-11)
- [x] `core/jobs/join_codes.go` runs it once at API start-up, each class in its own transaction
      that takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(73819, 41)` and re-reads the code row; no session lock.
      It logs the count, applies, logs `legacy_join_codes_rotated`; a second run finds nothing.
      The key is recorded in `20-data-model.md`'s advisory-lock registry (10 and 11 imports, 40
      maintenance windows, 41 this rotation, 42 R7's notification scheduler). A class whose
      transaction is a deadlock or serialization victim is retried once in the same run
- [x] Each affected teacher gets one `join_codes.rotated` notification `{count, classNames (≤5)}`
      targeting Classes; T-R4.41 shows the callout while it is unread
- [x] Legacy rows already revoked or expired are untouched; removing the legacy lookup branch is
      R5's contract step
- [x] Tests: a legacy code is rotated; the old code then answers as a code its teacher replaced
      does (`JOIN_CODE_REVOKED`) where self-join is open, and as an unknown code where it is
      closed or the class archived, before and after; the
      new code joins; two concurrent start-ups rotate once; the audit row and the notification
      exist; the R3 binary redeems the new code (R2 shipped the encrypted lookup)
- [ ] The count on a Neon branch copy of production is recorded for the release note

**Depends on:** —
**Touches:** `server/internal/platform/ratelimit/{registry.go,principal.go (new)}`, `server/internal/platform/ratelimit/tests/`, `server/internal/platform/httpx/ratelimit.go`, `server/internal/platform/httpx/publicroutes.go` (the start-up assertion, tested directly as `AssertPublicRoutesLimited` is), `server/internal/platform/httpx/tests/middleware_language_test.go`, `server/internal/core/router/{router.go,deps.go,ratelimits.go}`, `server/internal/core/core.go`, `server/internal/core/router/tests/{router_test.go,credential_limits_test.go}`, `server/internal/core/router/tests/public_routes_test.go` and `permissions_test.go` (`TestRateLimitsRunBeforeAuthenticationAndThePermissionGate` moves to `POST /app/classes/join`, where the forbidden caller is a teacher), `AGENTS.md` (the middleware note)
**Size:** S
**As built:**
- **Touched beyond the list:** `platform/httpx/publicroutes.go` (the start-up assertion),
  `platform/httpx/tests/{permission_test.go,middleware_language_test.go,principal_ratelimit_test.go (new)}`,
  `platform/ratelimit/tests/principal_test.go` (new),
  `core/router/tests/{principal_limits_test.go (new),permissions_test.go,public_routes_test.go}`,
  `docs/quizzivy-spec-v0.3.md` (v0.48) and, in `AGENTS.md`, the `core/router` row of the
  composition-root table, which now names the three registries. `core/router/deps.go`,
  `core/core.go` and `router_test.go` needed no change: `router.New` builds both registries
  itself.
- **The names.** `ratelimit.WithPrincipal` and the `KeyFunc` `ratelimit.PrincipalKey`;
  `Registry.AddKeyed(pattern)`, a route with no per-address bucket;
  `httpx.PrincipalRateLimit(reg)`; `httpx.AssertPrincipalRoutesGated(spec, scheme, reg)`;
  `router.PrincipalRateLimits()`. A later task adds one line to that function:
  `perActor("PUT /me/avatar", ratelimit.PerHour(10))`.
- **The key is the resolved principal's user id** (`Principal.Access.UserID`), which
  `RequirePermission` puts on the context, never the token's subject. A request with no resolved
  principal passes and spends nothing.
- **The five moved and left `RateLimits()`**, with their numbers. An anonymous or forbidden
  caller of one is answered 401 or 403 every time and spends no budget; a caller who holds the
  permission has one budget wherever they connect from.
- **"Open" is the gate's rule.** The assertion refuses an entry on any operation that does not
  require `bearerAuth`: all of `theOpenSeven`, refresh and logout included, which
  `AssertPublicRoutesLimited` does not count as public. It also refuses an entry that names no
  operation.
- **`TestRateLimitsRunBeforeAuthenticationAndThePermissionGate` changed its operation**, to
  `POST /app/classes/join` (120 a minute per address), and its forbidden caller to a teacher,
  because a student holds `learning.take_tests`. Its claim is the same.
- **The start-up call is pinned by reading `router.go`.**
  `TestTheRouterAssertsThePrincipalRegistryItEnforces` requires the registry asserted to be the
  one enforced, and `TestTheRouterStopsOnThePrincipalAssertionsError` requires `New` to return
  the assertion's error. `router.New` cannot be handed a bad registry, so no request shows
  either.
- **No contract change.** None of the five declares `x-rate-limit`, and none declares a 429
  response; both were so before this task. A per-actor operation takes no `x-rate-limit`
  block: `TestEveryRateLimitBlockMatchesTheRegistry` reads `RateLimits()` only and pins seven.
- **Spec v0.48.** §6.5 lists the five budgets per signed-in user, and §5.5 says it of
  `openDocsSession`. `72-r2.md` keeps "per address" (T-R2.8, T-R2.14b) as R2's record.
**Done when:**
- [x] `ratelimit.PrincipalKey` keys a bucket by the resolved principal's user id.
      `PrincipalRateLimits()` is a second registry beside `RateLimits()`; an entry for an open
      operation fails at start-up, since no principal exists there
- [x] A second limiter middleware reads it, placed directly after `RequirePermission` in
      `router.New`'s `inExecutionOrder` list; `RateLimits()` keeps running first, keyed by
      address or body field, before authentication. A router test pins both positions, as `validate_test.go` and
      `hardening_test.go` pin the order today
- [x] Every limit this release gives "per user" or "per actor" is registered here (T-R4.8,
      T-R4.18, T-R4.20). The authenticated credential minters R2 keyed per address
      (resetStudentPassword, createStudent, rotateJoinCode, getJoinCode, openDocsSession) move

- [x] `theCredentialMinters` accepts an entry in either registry; `TestRegistryHasNoStaleEntries`
      covers both

- [x] Tests: two actors behind one address keep separate budgets; one actor from two addresses
      shares one; a 429 answers as the address limiter's does
